There’s something aesthetically pleasing about their contrasts, too, with Kara’s crisp white dress shirt against Lena’s black off-the-shoulder dress and her blonde hair – worn down in soft waves rather than her trademark ponytail, another distracting element of the situation – next to Lena’s black. Their reflection looks undeniably couple-ey, and Kara of course looks completely comfortable and unaware of the way Lena’s palms have started to sweat.

    “Thank you,” Lena croaks, her mouth suddenly dry. Kara shifts, but doesn’t move away.

    “We can always cancel.”

    “I can handle it,” Lena assures her, clearing her throat and folding her hands on the desk in front of her. “Besides, I don’t want to have gotten all dressed up for nothing. I put on perfume and everything.”

    “Yeah, what is that? You smell amazing.” Blithely, Kara picks up Lena’s wrist and brings it to her face, breathing in softly. “And very expensive.”

    The tip of Kara’s nose brushes the delicate skin of Lena’s wrist, and Lena feels it echoing in decidedly un-platonic places.

    “Dior,” Lena says faintly, all her breath seeming to leave her just when she needs it most. For a brief, thrilling moment Lena almost thinks Kara might press a kiss there, but she doesn’t – she just grins, setting Lena’s hand back down with a gallant flourish.

    “I like it.”

    “Thank you,” Lena repeats, feeling like a parrot but seemingly unable to come up with anything more clever. Kara doesn’t seem to mind; she abruptly pivots the conversation, pulling out her phone as if the moment they just shared wasn’t one of the most intimate Lena has had in years.

    “We should take a picture.”

    “For posterity?” Lena says, straightening her shoulders and managing to put a little distance between them. Her heart is still racing, and she has to school the slight tremble out of her voice. “To remember the night of our first fake double date?”

    “For Instagram,” Kara corrects, holding the phone up to capture their combined reflection.

    “Ah. Of course.” An uncomfortable spike of disappointment shoots through Lena, but she masks it with a practiced smile – a smile which Kara immediately lets her know makes her look like she’s being held at gunpoint. Kara only gets the picture she’s looking for after putting concentrated effort into making her laugh, captioning it with can you believe this is my date for tonight? with a cavalcade of heart-eyed emojis, and only then does she finally move away. She heads over to the TV stand to flip through the room service menu while she waits for Lena to finish getting ready, but it turns out that Lena’s hands are now too shaky to mess with her makeup any more than she already has.

    After following Kara’s advice to snap a quick photo for Lena’s Instagram as well – Jess has been hounding her ever since the interview to use it more, and Kara seems to be an expert at artful and vaguely romantic selfies – they head to the restaurant.

    It’s much more upscale than anywhere they’ve been together before, an experimental modernist cuisine spot where Kara claims that many a celebrity date has been photographed, and thus much more suited to strategic press coverage than Kara’s usual hidden gems. There are a few photographers outside already when they climb out of the car, and Kara slips her hand easily into Lena’s just in time for a few flashes to go off before they duck inside.

    The interior is dark, black walls and drapery with dim lighting that makes Lena feel like she should have worn glasses instead of contacts, and the hostess leads them to a table near the windows where Alex and Kelly are already waiting. Both stand to greet them, Kelly giving Lena a warm hug and Alex offering a firm but slightly awkward handshake, before settling in for stilted chit-chat while the hostess rattles off the list of specials.

    “So,” Alex drawls once the waiter has taken their drink orders and disappeared, “couple lessons. I’m not really sure what we can do to help you.”

    “What Alex means to say is that we’re flattered you’d come to us,” Kelly interrupts, giving her an admonishing look. Alex makes a face.

    “Well, you guys are the best couple I know,” Kara says, but before Kelly can thank her for the compliment Alex speaks up again.

    “And we’re two of the four people who know the truth of your situation.”

    “That, too,” Kara says easily. She doesn’t seem bothered by Alex’s attitude at all, and Lena moves a little closer to her – maybe if she really tries, she can absorb some of that confidence. It seems to work when they perform, after all.

    “I’m very grateful for any tips you can share on being a little more…effortless,” Lena says, pushing past her hesitance. It’s not fair to put this all on Kara when they’re supposed to be in this together. “This sort of thing doesn’t come naturally to me. I can handle press conferences and board rooms, but generating positive press is a different skillset.”

    “I think you’re doing great,” Kara says encouragingly, slinging her arm over the back of Lena’s chair. Kelly’s eyes flick down to the movement, her smile turning thoughtful, and Lena clenches her hands together in her lap.

    “I appreciate that. But much like our dance partnership, you’re the one doing most of the work in this department.”

    “All you needed there was a little encouragement and some practice,” Kara counters, giving Lena’s shoulder a squeeze. “I have full confidence that this’ll be the same.”

    Lena smiles, Kara’s arm and her warm smile settling her nerves a bit, and when she turns back to Alex and Kelly she finds them sharing a significant look.

    “What?” Kara asks, her arm relaxing but not leaving the back of Lena’s chair. Alex shrugs innocently.

    “What, what?”

    Kara frowns, looking between them suspiciously. She looks as unconvinced as Lena feels. “You’re being weird.”

    “You asked us to come here to teach you how to be a fake couple, and you think I’m being weird?” Alex snorts, leaning forward to brace her arms on the table. “You’re the ones who -”

    “Sweetie,” Kelly says, putting a gentle hand on Alex’s thigh; as Lena has seen her do a few times before, Alex relaxes immediately.

    “Alright,” Alex sighs, leaning back again until her chair tips off the ground a little. “Sorry. In my opinion, the first thing you two should do is get to know each other properly.”

    “We do know each other!” Kara protests, indignant.

    “You’re trying to simulate a relationship. People will expect you to know intimate details that you haven’t even thought about, and you probably don’t want to be caught not knowing,” Alex says, and while to Lena it sounds perfectly reasonable, Kara seems offended by the assumption.

    “Like what?”

    “Like where you both grew up. Respective birthdays, names of family members.”

    “Born in Ireland and grew up in upstate New York, October 24th, hates talking about her family to the press. Give me something hard,” Kara fires back. Alex blinks, but Lena is pretty sure she’s not the most surprised person at the table.

    “How did you know my birthday?” Lena asks, turning fully towards Kara. Kara shrugs, folding one leg loosely over the other so that her ankle rests on her knee – her foot is bouncing quickly enough to power a small generator, but besides that she seems completely calm.

    “You told me when I made your Instagram account. Mine is August 15th.”

    “Okay, then more granular things,” Alex tries again, listing a few things off on her fingers. “Favourite foods, favourite colours.”

    “Potstickers and red,” Lena blurts, at the same time as Kara says “Sushi and blue.”

    “Remind me why we’re even here?” Alex says to Kelly, clearly exasperated. Kelly chuckles and leans into her wife until Alex automatically opens up her arm, and Kelly snuggles into her body.

    “I think what they’re really asking for help with is mannerisms. Public displays of affection, intimacy. Appropriate use of touch.”

    In truth, Lena thinks, they probably need help with both departments. But the latter is the one that makes her stomach lurch with anxiety. Kara makes it look so easy, but every time Lena thinks about reaching out for any kind of casual contact in the public eye it feels stilted and clumsy. It isn’t that she doesn’t want to touch Kara – it’s that she wants it too much. She wants it so much that it feels like Kara must be able to see it broadcast in every brush of their shoulders, every squeeze of the hand, and here she is asking to learn how to show it to the entire world.

    The waiter chooses that moment to deliver their drinks and ask for their orders, and while everyone lists off their food requests Lena drains her wineglass in a few gulps.

    “Hey. You okay?” Kara asks quietly once the waiter has left again, leaning close enough for Lena to smell cinnamon. Lena nods despite everything else in her body saying otherwise.

    “I just need to use the restroom.”

    What she needs is a brief escape from the conversation, but she’s foiled when Alex stands up from the table with her.

    “I’ll join you,” Alex says, folding her lap napkin neatly and setting it on the table, and Lena follows her to the bathrooms fighting not to show her disappointment. Alex doesn’t go into a stall when they enter, instead checking them for possible eavesdroppers, and Lena leans heavily against the sink.

    “Is this where I get the talk?”

    “You really are as smart as Kara says you are,” Alex says, glancing into the final stall with a satisfied nod, but there’s no malice in it. She seems genuine, and she also seems to want to do this as little as Lena does.

    “And you really are as protective as she says you are,” Lena counters, folding her arms tightly over her chest. “I assume you’re here to tell me not to hurt her?”

    Alex nods, her own arms crossing in a mirrored gesture. “More or less. This whole…situation is a potential forest fire.” Alex’s leather-booted toe taps incessantly against the stone tile, a fidget so similar to Kara’s that it almost settles Lena’s nerves to see it.

    “You know she’s the one who suggested it, right?” Lena says, unable to stop the slightly defensive tone. “I wanted to just come clean and face the press fallout, but she insisted.”

    Alex seems unsurprised. She leans against the counter next to Lena, staring resolutely ahead rather than looking at her directly.

    “I know. Kara has always put other people before herself, and she’s decided that she wants to help you. Just…be careful with her, okay?”

    “I care about her,” Lena says hotly, her conflict-aversion falling away at the assumption that she’d ever hurt Kara. “I’d never willingly –”

    “I’m not saying it would be willingly,” Alex says, managing to remain kind but unquestionably firm. “Because she’s so positive, she has a way of making people think that nothing bothers her. But she’s a lot more sensitive than she seems. I just…feel like she’s putting too much of herself into this, and I need you to promise me that if it’s getting too intense, you’ll put a stop to it before she gets totally crushed. She’d rather push the pain down forever than tell you she can’t handle it anymore.”

    By the time she’s finished Alex is looking directly at Lena, and it feels as if she’s trying to convey a meaning beyond her words. One that Lena can understand, but seems so ridiculous that she’s not sure how to reply.

    “If you’re talking about PR, I’m going to do everything I can to make sure she comes out of this pristinely,” Lena says, finally breaking their wall-staring stalemate and meeting her gaze. “Her reputation is more important to me than my own.”

    Alex’s face is flushed red, and she rolls her head back with a groan.

    “Shit. Kelly told me this wouldn’t work, but I can’t believe she really found someone as emotionally constipated as she is.”

    “Excuse me?”

    “Never mind,” Alex grumbles, pushing off from the counter and stalking over to the door. “Just try not to fuck this up, okay?” She disappears without another word, and Lena is left staring into the space she left trying to dissect her meaning.

    She never does end up using the restroom.

    She returns to the table a few minutes later, and as soon as she sits down Kara squeezes her hand. Lena flashes her a brief smile – as thrown-off and uncomfortable as she is, the last thing she wants is for Kara to think it’s her fault. The rest of dinner passes quickly, while Lena hardly touches her food – it’s some kind of herb and salmon foam concoction that she pointed to on the menu without really looking, and even Kara makes a face when she tries a bite of it from Lena’s spoon.

    Kara excuses herself to the restroom while the bills are being paid, and when Alex rises with her – seeming to actually need to use it, this time – Kara looks at her questioningly.

    “Didn’t you just go with Lena?”

    “I had a lot of coffee today,” Alex deflects, nudging Kara as she walks by. “Can’t a woman pee in peace?”

    They leave the table bickering all the way to the washroom, and in their absence Kelly’s attention turns to Lena.

    “That went about as well as it was ever going to, I think,” Kelly says, all exasperated fondness. Kelly must have years of experience with Alex and Kara, and she doesn’t seem worried – it’s enough for Lena to relax a little. Maybe this didn’t go quite as badly as she thought.

    “You’re not the one who was interrogated in the bathroom,” Lena mutters, glancing back at the distant bathroom door. Kelly laughs.

    “I told her that wouldn’t work.”

    “She mentioned,” Lena says drily. “I appreciate that she’s so protective, but I think she has entirely the wrong idea about Kara’s intentions.”

    Kelly doesn’t answer that. She takes a sip of her martini, looking thoughtfully at Lena, and Lena can’t hold her gaze for more than a few seconds. It feels like Kelly can see everything she’s trying to hide, somehow, and the kindness of her expression doesn’t stop the discomfort it causes.

    “Honestly I’m not sure you two needed to learn anything from us,” Kelly says finally, setting her drink down again. “From what I can see, you’re naturals.”

    “That’s very kind,” Lena says with a light laugh, swirling the remnants of her wine around the bottom of the glass. “But you don’t have to lie. I haven’t been in a relationship in years. I’m fully aware of my shortcomings in that department.”

    Kelly smiles enigmatically. “You could have fooled me.”

    Kara and Alex reappear then, still bantering back and forth, and Lena has never been more relieved to be free of a social engagement than she is when the paid bills come back and Kara suggests it’s time to go. Alex and Kelly see them off with a promise to do it again soon and a printed list of 50 questions to ask in a new relationship, and for the second time they retire to Lena’s hotel room together.

    “Allergies?” Kara says, lying flat on her back on the floor. She’s still in her dinner clothes and one of her legs is crossed over the other, her polka-dot-socked foot in the air wiggling to some unheard music that Lena assumes is playing in her head at all times, and she has the paper so close to her face that it flutters every time she breathes out.

    “Apples,” Lena replies from her place reclined on the pillows. “They make my mouth tingle. And dust. You?”

    “None that I know of. Eliza used to joke I had a superhuman immune system.”

    Kara squints at the paper, her face scrunched up in concentration, and Lena rolls slightly to get a better look at her struggle.

    “Do you want a magnifying glass?” Lena drawls. Kara tries to pin her with a glare, but the effect is dampened by her squinting.

    “Shush. My contacts are drying out.”

    “I didn’t know you wore contacts.”

    “It’s harder to dance in glasses,” Kara explains, giving up on her attempts at reading and letting the paper flutter down onto her face. “But I’ve usually swapped them out by now. My eyes hurt.”

    “I have contact solution and an extra case in the bathroom.”

    Kara sits up straight, the paper fluttering to the carpet. “That would be amazing! You don’t wear contacts, do you?”

    “In the interest of full disclosure,” Lena answers, holding up her copy of the 50 questions, “I do. Mine are getting dry as well, actually. I need to switch to my glasses.”

    “I don’t have mine with me,” Kara complains, getting up off the floor in a smooth movement and heading to the bathroom. “Good thing I’m not biking home.”

    “Names of family members – everyone already knows my family, I think we can skip that one,” Lena calls towards the open bathroom door. She can just barely see the edge of Kara’s reflection while she uncaps the solution. “I don’t think you’ll be expected to know anything about my birth mother when all I know is her name.”

    “What was her name?” Kara calls back, still attentive even while she fishes her contacts out. She’s less efficient at it than Lena usually is, and it takes her three tries before she successfully gets them into Lena’s spare case.

    “Elizabeth Walsh.”

    “Alura Eldon,” Kara offers in response. “My father’s name was Zor. My only other blood relation is my cousin Clark,” Kara pokes her head back into the room again, one of her eyes squeezed shut. “He’s actually married to Lucy’s older sister. Do you have any eye drops?”

    “In my makeup bag. Didn’t you date Lucy?” Lena asks, and Kara’s voice echoes back immediately as she searches for the bottle.

    “They didn’t get together until after Luce and I broke up,” Kara says loudly. She applies the drops quickly, sighing in relief as she blinks the excess away and wipes at her eyes. “They met at work, so it’s not weird. Just a coincidence.”

    “Okay, so it’s not incestuous,” Lena concedes, grinning. This is clearly not the first time Kara has heard this, and it’s fun to push her buttons a little. “Just worryingly insular.”

    “Hey, you’re the one publicly entering that dating pool,” Kara says, finally coming fully back into the room with a finger pointed at her. Lena makes a thoughtful face.

    “Is that what I agreed to?”

    “Should have read the fine print,” Kara says in a sing-song voice, and while Lena laughs she takes a seat at the end of the bed near Lena’s feet rather than lying on the floor again. She grabs at Lena’s copy of the questionnaire, moving to the next number. “Okay, next up – what’s your comfort movie?”

    “Define comfort movie.”

    “You know, the one you watch when you need to be cheered up. Your sick-day movie,” Kara says, as if the term is self-explanatory. “Childhood favourite.”

    Lena shrugs. “What’s yours?”

    “The Wizard of Oz.”

    “Cute,” Lena says, grinning at that mental image – a tiny Kara huddled up in front of a boxy old TV, watching the flying monkeys with rapt attention. Kara only looks at her pointedly, still awaiting her answer, and Lena sighs.

    “Star Wars.”

    Kara’s expression can only be described as unadulterated glee. The smile overtakes her face slowly but surely - Lena rolls her eyes, grabbing the nearest pillow and throwing it in Kara’s direction.

    “Don’t.”

    “Oh my god,” Kara says, catching the pillow easily and tucking it under her arm. “Is Lena Luthor a secret nerd?”

    “Those films kick-started a new era in science fiction –” Lena starts, nudging Kara with her foot, but Kara’s delight is too much to contain.

    “I’m not making fun of you, Lena! I love this,” Kara exclaims, and Lena has no reason to doubt her truthfulness. “I was expecting…I don’t know, something else. But Star Wars, that’s so awesome!”

    “I wanted to be an astronaut when I was a child,” Lena says, still a little defensive. “It was a movie about space with a badass princess in it.”

    “Hey, that’s question 26! Childhood aspiration. Jeez, Alex, clinical much?” Kara mutters, flipping to that page and frowning at the wording. “I wanted to be a superhero.”

    Lena raises an eyebrow. It takes Kara a moment to notice, but when she looks up from the paper to see Lena’s expression she grins, shrugging.

    “What? Nobody said it had to be realistic!”

    “This explains so much about you,” Lena says with a chuckle. Kara flops over and props herself up on an elbow with the pilfered pillow underneath, now draped over the entire foot of the bed and looking at Lena questioningly.

    “What do you mean?”

    “How many times have you swooped to my rescue? Look at the situation we’re in now,” Lena says, gesturing around the room. Kara is lying partially on her feet, and Lena is careful not to move them. “Even becoming my partner on this show in the first place was because you wanted to help me.”

    Kara frowns. She looks confused, and Lena wonders if Kara even realizes how apt her childhood aspiration is. “Is that a bad thing?”

    “Not necessarily,” Lena edges, remembering Alex’s uncomfortable words from earlier. She had dismissed it at the time, but now that she’s actually thinking about it, she knows Alex is right – Kara has been prioritizing Lena since they met. She’s spent weeks putting all her effort into making Lena feel comfortable, defended her to Leslie, and now has gone so far as to enter into a fake relationship to rescue her reputation. “But it’s not a bad thing to put yourself first sometimes, either.”

    “Question 31 – religious affiliation,” Kara says, turning onto her back and flipping to a new page without a reply. The topic is clearly closed, and Lena tries not to take it too personally. “Jewish. Converted. You?”

    “None.”

    “Really? Never even baptized?” Kara’s tone is still playful, not tense, and it eases Lena’s worry.

    “I’m not sure what my mother was,” Lena concedes, shrugging. “I might have been, at some point. But the Luthors…the closest thing Lex ever had to a bible was Ayn Rand. Although at one point I think he thought he was a god.”

    Kara winces, letting out a low whistle. “Not a source of comfort, then.”

    “Not so much. What about you? Did you convert when you were adopted, or before?”

    Kara doesn’t seem eager to answer that. She averts her eyes back to the page, shifting to get herself more comfortable, but in the process her elbow brushes the side of Lena’s foot just gently enough that Lena twitches hard. As much as she’s been enjoying Kara’s warm weight on her feet, she moves them away before Kara can accidentally tickle her again.

    Kara pauses.

    “Lena,” Kara says, sounding more delighted by the second, “how ticklish are you?”

    Or perhaps not so accidentally. Kara has found ticklish spots before, especially in Lena’s sides when she steadies her after lifts, but Lena usually manages to tamp down her reactions - this time, Kara caught her in a weak moment. Lena curls up slightly, getting her feet further from Kara. “That’s not on the list.”

    “It is, actually! Number 42, look.” Kara points to the paper and Lena looks at her own list, frowning - and while her attention is diverted Kara grabs her feet, tickling the bottoms mercilessly.

    “Kara!” Lena shrieks, flailing out and just barely managing not to kick her in the face. “I swear to god –”

    “I thought you weren’t religious?” Kara counters playfully, moving up Lena’s calves and finding the ticklish spot behind her knees. It’s a light sensation, and Kara seems to be feeling out whether Lena is enjoying it or not. “Which god are you swearing to, exactly?”

    “Not the point – Kara! This was not on the list!”

    Kara is relentless. She seems to feed on Lena’s laughter, moving up and tickling Lena’s sides now – she isn’t rough, doing it just hard enough to make Lena squirm and giggle, and she seems more pleased by Lena’s loss of decorum than by the tickling itself.

    “Kara, stop -” Lena finally gasps, laughing wildly while Kara’s hands find all her most sensitive spots; and the moment she says it, the tickling halts as if Kara was only waiting for her to ask. Lena manages to catch her breath, her chest aching in the pleasant sort of way that comes after laughter, and it’s then that she realizes Kara is on top of her.

    It isn’t only that they’re physically close. The pillows are fluffed all around them like a barrier from the outside world and Kara is positioned between Lena’s legs, Lena apparently at some point having half-wrapped them around Kara’s hips in an attempt to squirm away. Kara is braced above her, the top three buttons of her shirt undone to reveal the edge of the white tank top underneath, and Lena suddenly wishes Kara hadn’t stopped at all.

    “Sorry,” Kara says, breathless. She seems to realize their position belatedly, and without another word she rolls off the bed.

    “It’s late,” Kara says abruptly, landing smoothly on her feet. “I should go. Rehearsal tomorrow, right?” She starts gathering her things, shoving her feet back into her shoes and stuffing the folded questionnaire into her pocket while Lena tries to slow down her thundering heartbeat.

    “Right,” Lena says, dislodging the pillows and grabbing her phone. She runs a shaky hand through her hair, and she can tell just by how it feels through her fingers that it’s in total disarray. “I’ll call a driver.”

    “Don’t worry about it, I’ll just walk home,” Kara insists, fussing with one of her shoes – the heel of the leather has folded down in her rush, and while Lena dials Kara starts to half-hop towards the door. She seems embarrassed, in a hurry to leave, and while Lena understands the sentiment she doesn’t know how to tell her there’s nothing to be embarrassed over.

    “There’s no rush, it’ll only take a moment.”

    Kara grabs for the doorknob. “I really should leave you to get some sleep, I’ve been bothering you way too long -”

    “Kara. It’s over an hour’s walk, it’s dark out, and you can barely see. Don’t be ridiculous,” Lena says firmly, almost snapping at her in a last attempt to get Kara to calm down. Shockingly, it works; Kara stops in her tracks. Her cheeks are pink, but she waits quietly while Lena talks to the driver and hangs up.

    “The car will be downstairs when you get there.”

    “Thanks,” Kara nods, finally opening the door into the hallway much more calmly after fixing her shoe issue, and she’s halfway to the elevator and waving goodbye when Lena spots something.

    “Kara, wait!” she calls, grabbing it and darting back to the door before it closes. “Your contacts.”

    “Oh! Right,” Kara calls back, squinting at her and jogging back down the hall. She looks abashed when she takes the case from Lena, but she doesn’t sprint away again at least. “Thanks. And, uh…sorry again.”

    Kara seems so repentant for something Lena doesn’t consider an imposition. Kara has casually touched her in a hundred ways since they met, and been downright intimately close to her while dancing – she isn’t sure if it’s the tickling or the position they wound up in that has Kara’s gentlemanly sensibilities so twisted up, but in a last attempt to soothe her Lena puts a hand on her upper arm and squeezes gently.

    “We’ve been closer during routines. It’s perfectly fine.”

    Lena isn’t sure what comes over her in the moments after. Her blood is still pumping from the whole situation, Kara’s arm warm and firm under her hand, and something about Kara being so flustered makes her feel brave in comparison.

    She leans forward and presses a dark red lipstick kiss mark to the collar of Kara’s shirt, smudging it slightly for effect with her thumb.

    “For the press,” Lena says quietly, straightening the collar to be more visible. “They’re probably outside.”

    Kara lets out a shaky breath.

    “Good idea,” Kara says, swallowing hard and starting down the hall again only when Lena’s hand falls away. “Uh, see you tomorrow?”

    Kara takes the stairs this time, and Lena can hear her rapid footfalls echoing for a few moments before the door swings closed. It sounds like she’s bolting away, so desperate to put distance between them that she can’t even wait for the elevator.

    Lena closes her own door slowly, leaning heavily against it after she turns the lock. The bed is still a mess, and Lena climbs into it without even straightening it up - she has other things on her mind.

     

     

    Kara makes no mention of her rapid exit the next morning at rehearsal, and Lena sees no reason to bring it up.

    Lena’s assumption about the paparazzi turned out to be correct – there were a few photographers waiting outside the hotel, and in Jess’ daily update email Lena sees three mentions to the lipstick on Kara’s collar. In fact there seem to be photographers almost everywhere they go now with the exception of Kara’s apartment since Kara has gotten exceedingly good at shaking them on her bike ride home, and in a mutual decision they agree to keep their time together to public places or Lena’s hotel to preserve one tiny slice of Kara’s privacy. It’s necessary and Kara deserves far more than such a tiny sliver of discretion, but Lena misses the place even though she’s only been there once. Their night there had been so nice, and even though she enjoys spending any time with Kara she can manage, she wishes it wasn’t always coloured by the fact that they’re usually being observed.

    And observed they surely are. The flash of a camera becomes familiar in the week leading up to their fourth performance, and with each day Lena gets more comfortable with Kara’s touch. By Friday she’s stopped tensing up every time, and on Saturday night when Kara takes her to a bar to see her friend’s band perform and spends the whole evening wrapped around her from behind to protect her from the rowdy audience around them, Lena actually leans into it gratefully.

    It gets harder for Lena to tell herself that she just likes having an actual friend here, someone to have meals and watch movies with, when Kara is touching her. It’s when Kara leans close at dinner to talk in her ear or kiss her cheek that her self-delusion regarding the degree of her attraction weakens the most; Kara never takes it further, sticking religiously to the boundaries they set up together earlier in the week, but Lena fights with herself every time.

    Sam arrives on Sunday evening with an itemized list of possible pitfalls, and the fact that she and Kara get along almost as soon as they’re seated at the restaurant is both a relief and a curse.

    “I didn’t expect it to be going so well, but you guys have been selling it so far. Although it has only been a week.” Sam hardly waits for their drinks to arrive before getting down to business, and Lena wonders just how many uncomfortable dinners she’s going to be forced to endure before her tenure on the show is over.

    “True – but it’s been a good week,” Kara counters, leaning back on the back legs of her chair. They chose a much less upscale restaurant this time after the salmon-mousse incident, and Kara is clearly more at ease in this bistro. “And you don’t have to worry, I’m making sure Lena’s image comes first. If we screw something up, the blame is going on me.”

    “What? We didn’t agree to that,” Lena says, frowning at Kara’s precarious position. She tugs at Kara’s sleeve, but Kara doesn’t budge.

    “Your reputation has a whole company riding on it,” Kara says, shrugging. “And after everything you’ve been through with the press I’m not going to let you come under fire again if I can help it.”

    Lena raises a brow. “That’s unfortunate, since I already promised your sister I’d do everything I could to make sure your career comes first.”

    Kara rocks back a little in surprise, almost losing her balance, before all four of her chair legs finally touch the ground again. “Well, why would you do that?”

    “Have you ever been on the receiving end of your sister’s shotgun talk?” Lena says drily, swirling her scotch in its short glass. Kara, as always, protests.

    “She’s not that scary,” Kara says, pushing forward when Lena scoffs. “She still sleeps with a teddy bear!”

    “That doesn’t mean she wouldn’t eviscerate me with her pinkie finger if anything ever -”

    Sam clears her throat pointedly. When they both turn to her she’s looking back and forth between them with a smile that looks a little too smug for Lena’s liking, and Lena can feel herself starting to blush when she realizes she’d entirely forgotten Sam was there. And Sam clearly knows it.

    “The point is, I really think we’re going to pull this off,” Kara finishes, ever-confident and seeming unfazed. Sam smiles enigmatically.

    “Honestly, half the reason I’m here is to see you two interacting in person. So far I’ve only seen the media circus.”

    “And?” Lena asks, hoping that Sam picks up on her undertone. Please, for once in your life, be subtle.

    Thankfully Sam either understands or she’s feeling more reserved than usual. She takes a sip of her martini, plucking out the olive and popping it into her mouth. “I’ll give you my findings later.”

    Kara takes it in stride. She grins, stretching her arms above her head and letting one come to rest on the back of Lena’s chair. It’s a familiar gesture at this point, but in front of Sam it feels different somehow. Sam knows her too well to be fooled by Lena’s flimsy acting skills.

    “Fair enough. You should come to the cast party with us after the show!” Kara suggests, clearly thinking she’s being helpful. “See how we do in a group setting. We haven’t really hung out with other people yet as a couple, besides my sister and her wife. And you, obviously.”

    Something twists deep in Lena’s chest at that wording. As a couple. Sam notices, too – Lena can see a softening in her face, almost pity in her eyes. And the pity is worse than the doubt. Lena turns away from her, focusing on Kara instead.

    “We’ve mostly focused on getting to know each other,” Lena adds, taking a larger-than-necessary mouthful of scotch. The comfortable burn of it is grounding, which is especially helpful considering Sam’s next words.

    “Yeah, my sister gave us this list of questions – it was weird, but it worked. I know everything about Lena now,” Kara says with an easy wink, raising her beer up to take a sip.

    “Like all her secret tattoo locations?” Sam says lightly.

    Kara sprays beer clear across the table.

    So much for reserved; Sam, apparently having anticipated this, manages to scoot her chair over to avoid the splash zone. The same can’t be said for the basket of bread in the middle of the table. It gets soaked as Kara coughs up beer, gasping and red-faced while Lena hurries to help her. It takes a few seconds for her to catch her breath again, and Lena glares at Sam across the table with all the severity she can muster.

    “Sam,” Lena hisses, rubbing Kara’s back in small, soothing circles.

    “Ooh! She doesn’t know everything, clearly.” Sam lays a napkin down over the wet tablecloth in front of her, setting her drink back down now that it’s not in danger of being cross-contaminated. “And it’s a fact that she should know about you if she’s supposed to have seen you naked. Were tattoos and piercings not on your list of questions?”

    “Oh my god,” Lena mutters, abandoning rubbing Kara’s back to bury her face in her hands. She’s fairly sure she’s never blushed so hard in her life, and Sam didn’t react to the glare at all. Probably because, much to Lena’s dismay, she’s probably right. She wears clothes that cover her various current and former body modifications for a reason, but she certainly hasn’t always done so - if they ever come up, Kara should know about them. As horribly uncomfortable as it feels.

    “No,” Kara wheezes, rubbing her chest, “I guess Alex didn’t think of that.”

    “This is why you need people on your team,” Sam says, finishing her drink and politely waving down a waitress for a new one.

    Kara turns to Lena, her airways finally clear and her eyes wide and attentive. “Uh. I’ve seen the one on your wrist, but…secret ones?”

    “On my hip,” Lena says shortly, pulling the band-aid off. “And my lower back.”

    “You have a lower back tattoo?”

    “She also had her tongue pierced,” Sam adds unhelpfully, grinning. “Among other things.”

    Lena glares at her again; Kara looks dazed.

    “Can I see them?”

    Lena blinks back at her. She glances around the restaurant, looking back at Kara incredulously.

    “Here?”

    Kara seems to come out of her daze - she looks around too like she’s only just remembered they’re in public, letting out a stilted laugh. “Oh. Right. Probably not the right place, huh?”

    Kara rubs at the back of her neck under her hair, clearing her throat and taking a much more careful gulp of her drink. The only one at the table not blushing is Sam, who’s instead looking at them rather delightedly over the rim of her second glass.

    Thankfully their food arrives soon after, and the atmosphere gets lighter while they eat. Now that she’s gotten it out of her system Sam seems to take a step back, making real conversation rather than focusing on them, and it’s a relief to finish the meal without any more incidents.

    “She was nice,” Kara says mildly once they’ve said their goodbyes until tomorrow, putting an arm around Lena’s waist while they walk – the restaurant is only a few blocks from Lena’s hotel, and Lena knew without asking that Kara would want to walk it rather than wait in traffic. Lena leans into her, chewing on her lip.

    “She certainly liked you.”

    “You think so?”

    “She wouldn’t have teased you otherwise,” Lena explains. Kara looks relieved. “If Sam doesn’t like someone, you can tell. She’d have treated the meal like a business meeting.”

    Kara grins, her steps a little more bouncy and confident. “Well, that’s good! I liked her too. I’m glad she agreed to come to the cast party.”

    “Prepare for her to make you uncomfortable again,” Lena grumbles.

    “I wasn’t uncomfortable.”

    Lena pins her with a doubtful look. “You inhaled your beer.”

    Kara laughs, moving her arm to instead tangle their hands together as they approach the hotel. “I told you, I was just surprised. I guess we’ll have to find some more questions to ask each other – clearly 50 wasn’t enough.”

    “You’re going to know me better than I know myself by the end of this,” Lena says, partially under her breath. Kara, already googling 100 questions to ask a new partner with her free hand, doesn’t seem bothered at all. They spend the rest of the evening quizzing each other; in a few hours Lena learns that Kara loves to paint, she can deadlift 300 pounds (whatever that means), and that although she doesn’t particularly want children she does apparently want to get married when she finds the right person.

    That last question is where Lena finally draws the evening to a close.

     

     

    Up until now, show day has fallen into a strange sort of routine. Lena knows where she needs to be and when, and she can follow her stage directions until the night is over without having to put too much thought into anything except her performance with Kara.

    Sam being present puts a slight wrench in the gears. She’s not in the way or anything – after a quick breakfast where Sam gave away absolutely none of her thoughts on the situation, Lena doesn’t even see her in the chaos of the day. But knowing she’s out in the audience somewhere, probably with a very good seat thanks to Kara vouching for her to get VIP treatment, is more nerve-wracking than knowing she’s been watching from home.

    The final costume fitting goes more quickly than usual – the outfit they have her in is a form-fitting dark purple number and reveals most of the left half of her torso – and it means that by the time Andrea, Siobhan, and Leslie file into the makeup trailer Lena is close to finishing up.

    “If it isn’t the talk of the town,” Leslie jeers just as the makeup artist is putting the cap on Lena’s lipstick, her voice as loud and brash as usual. “Enjoying your 15 minutes of fame, Luthor?”

    Lena ignores her. Leslie, clearly still not used to not getting a rise out of people, only pushes harder.

    “Be a shame if it all came crashing down, wouldn’t it?”

    Stuck in the chair until her hair tech is finished, Lena’s curiosity gets the better of her. “What are you talking about?”

    “Partner swap week is coming up. Next week you’re gonna have to dance with someone that isn’t Danvers, and we’ll all get to see just how much being her fuckbuddy has been keeping you here.”

    Lena’s stomach drops.

    She’s known that there was a partner swap week coming since the beginning – an outline of the season was given to each of them when they first arrived, and they’re heading into week 5. Week 5 is when the stars all switch up their professional dancers for one routine. But Lena had been so convinced that she was going home early that she hadn’t given it much thought since then, and Kara hasn’t brought it up either. She’d forgotten about it entirely until now.

    Leslie is right. Next week, she’s going to have to do this without Kara.

    “What is your problem?” Lena snaps, the sudden terror making her temper slipping from her grasp in the face of Leslie’s smug, self-satisfied expression. Leslie knew this would get under her skin, and she was right.

    “My problem is that you’ve been getting your toes sucked by the judges like you’re god’s gift to the stage just because she makes you look good.” There’s vitriol in Leslie’s tone, vitriol that Lena has no idea how she earned, but she’s saved from having to answer by Siobhan chipping in.

    “As much as I hate to compliment anyone besides myself, you have to admire the strategy.”

    “What strategy?” Lena asks, spitting the words out through clenched teeth. Siobhan scoffs.

    “Seducing your partner. Gets a way better performance out of them, clearly. Maybe I should try that.”

    Lena’s temper comes back with a vengeance. “I didn’t seduce Kara.”

    “Oh, please,” Siobhan sniffs, closing her eyes to let the artist do her eyeliner. “The way she trails after you? It’s clearly going well for you.”

    Lena is silent, seething. Andrea hasn’t said anything either, pointedly looking at her own reflection in the mirror rather than acknowledging anyone in the room, but her stoic expression is one Lena recognizes. She’s masking something. Not that Lena has any inclination to figure out what it is, right now.

    “Do you think Winn is the kind of man who finds fear an aphrodisiac?” Siobhan says thoughtfully, taking a sip of her coffee and leaving lipstick around the lid. “I have him terrified of me, but I do see him staring at my tits sometimes. How did you pull it off?”

    It’s just about as much as Lena can handle. She stands up, giving a quick muttered sorry to the hair tech and bolting from the trailer – she’s pretty sure the tech was finished anyways and was just enjoying the gossip, so her remorse is limited.

    The words partner swap echo in her head as she flees, seeking out Kara backstage like a moth to a flame. The moment she sees her, leaned against the wall in her costume chatting with Brainy and Nia, Lena’s stress eases a little.

    “Wow,” Kara says, brightening when she catches sight of Lena. “Look at you – you look amazing.”

    Kara herself looks much like she did for their dinner with Alex and Kelly in a white shirt tucked into black pants, and it has the same effect now as it did then. Butterflies erupt in Lena’s belly, and she lets herself be pulled into Kara’s side without any resistance.

    “You okay?” Kara whispers in her ear, disguising it with a light kiss on her hair. Lena exhales slowly.

    “Later.”

    Kara nods, and turns back to Nia and Brainy without missing a beat. “I was just telling Nia we’re going to wipe the floor with her tonight.”

    “You probably are,” Nia concedes, unbothered. “Barry wants to go home to his wife, so he has not been putting the work in.”

    Brainy jumps to talk Nia up, smitten as always. “I am sure your talent will pull you through.”

    “Not against these two,” Nia says, gesturing her thumb at them. “What do you guys have planned? Samba, right?”

    Alex’s head pops around the corner near Kara’s shoulder, her headset dangling off her ear. She doesn’t even have to say anything – all it takes is her presence for them to scatter to where they’re supposed to be mid-conversation. The madness of the broadcast sweeps them up, and Lena hardly has time to think before the four routines ahead of them have already finished and they’re up next.

    Their routine starts at the top of the steps near the back of the stage, with Lena draped against the wall and Kara waiting just in the wings to sweep in when the music starts. A few weeks ago Lena would have panicked at the idea of such a dramatic entrance hinging on her alone on stage, but now the crowd hardly phases her. Kara gives her a bright thumbs-up and when their video fades out into the first notes of the song all thoughts of the media and Leslie and swapping partners are put out of Lena’s mind.

    As always her world narrows to Kara and the music as soon as the blonde strides across the stage to take her hand. They walk down the stairs together to the beat, starting the choreography with matching smiles, and Kara is right there for her at every turn. Lena forgets Sam in the audience; she forgets what’s felt all week like the eyes of the world on them. In this liminal space, this precious two minutes where all she needs to worry about is executing the routine Kara crafted for them, Lena allows herself to sink into the fantasy in the same way that so terrified her last week.

    When Kara pulls her close, moving her hips suggestively while biting her lip, Lena improvises with a hand on her chest – she drags it across while she does the choreographed move of striding behind Kara for a spin, dragging open Kara’s shirt even further and stirring the crowd into a frenzy of cheers. When Kara twirls to follow her eyes are alight with passion, wide and delighted, and it pulls at something in Lena. Something that lets her melt into Kara’s arms at just the right times, that lets her act out the dramatic moments of emotion and desire that Kara has been encouraging her to tap into. The choreography is secondary, nothing more than a backdrop for their back-and-forth.

    It's fun, just like Kara hoped. Lena’s smile is genuine for the whole number, not forced, and when they end with an intimate assisted half-split that Lena has been working on for the entire week – and which she’s only managed to execute because Kara is actually supporting all of her weight, and is responsible for pulling her back up to standing again – it’s easy to tell that the audience has picked up on it.

    It might even be their loudest cheer yet. Even Brainy gives them a subtle encouraging grin when he leads them towards the judging table, and this week it’s Cat who speaks first.

    “I think it’s clear now to everyone watching this where your chemistry comes from.”

    The stage floor rattles with cheers. It even overpowers the music, and Cat has to wait a few moments before she can be heard again. “I’m not usually one to make predictions, but I truly hope you continue to harness it the way you have. I think it has the potential to carry you both all the way to the end.”

    Lena can feel Kara practically vibrating with glee beside her at Cat’s praise, their hands laced so tightly together that Lena’s starts to tingle with blood loss.

    “With every performance you get looser, Lena,” Jonn says in his deep rumble, calm and even in the face of the overwhelming energy of the crowd. “More comfortable on this stage. More joyful to watch. I know I focus on technique, and there were pitfalls - but it’s refreshing seeing two people just enjoy themselves through dance. That’s what it’s all about.”

    “I know I’m enjoying myself,” William says, riling the audience up yet again. Lena has to resist covering her ears against the noise. “I didn’t think anything could one-up your Disney routine last week, but you two came out here and proved to us that you just keep getting better. I have no idea when this momentum will run out, but I hope the answer is never!”

    Despite the positive reaction that comment gets, it makes Lena’s stomach lurch a little.

    It’s unsurprising even to Lena that they get top marks – two 10’s and a 9, putting them in the top spot for the week with Andrea and James sitting two points below and Nia and Barry getting sent home just as the younger woman predicted. Andrea is a consummate professional while the cameras are on, her smile perfectly affixed much as Lena is sure it always is when she’s reading the news, but once the broadcast ends and the cameras power down Lena catches her shooting a deadly glare in Kara’s direction while they all file backstage.

    The glare softens when she meets eyes with Lena, turning into something much more difficult to parse.

    Having promised to attend with Sam, Lena can’t justify skipping the afterparty this time. They arrive to it in full swing, the private booth fully loaded with bottle service, and while Lena sticks to Kara’s side Sam immediately branches out to integrate herself into whatever relationship dynamic is happening with half the cast; with a drink in each hand she dances with Mike and Imra, flirts mercilessly with Alex and Kelly, and barely an hour into the party Lena spots her in the corner of the booth making out with Sara Lance.

    “She seems to be having fun,” Kara notes, her hands resting on Lena’s shoulders. Idly Kara’s thumbs start to dig into the tight muscle, and the release of tension is so welcome that Lena groans quietly, her head rolling back and her eyes closing. It feels nice, a relief from the atmosphere around them, and she leans into it gladly.

    “Well, this is a development.”

    Sam’s voice appears over the music like a spectre just to Lena’s left, and she startles so hard that she knocks Kara’s hands away.

    “Jesus, Sam,” Lena snaps, her heart pounding. Kara stays where she is, but her hands don’t reclaim their spot on Lena’s shoulders. “I thought you were busy going home with Kara’s coworkers?”

    “All in good time,” Sam says, scooting into the booth beside Lena to make another drink. Kara falls into conversation with Lucy on the other side of the table, and while she’s distracted Sam leans close for a sidebar. “Lena, you’ve refused to get a masseuse for the entire time I’ve known you.”

    “…and?”

    “And you’re letting Kara massage you,” Sam points out, mixing far too much vodka into a truly pitiful amount of mixer and taking a sip without flinching.

    “Kara isn’t a stranger,” Lena clarifies. “I don’t like strangers touching me.”

    “Would you let me massage you?” Sam reaches out her hands in a grabby motion, going for Lena’s shoulders, and Lena squirms away.

    “Exactly,” Sam says more gently, her hands retreating back to her drink. “Just like everything else, it makes you uncomfortable unless it’s her.”

    “Does that mean we sold it?” Lena asks, fiddling with the straw in her own mostly-untouched drink. “Can I stop hearing about how bad an idea this is?”

    Sam sighs, catching Sara’s eye out on the dance floor. “Fine. But I reserve the right to say I told you so.”

    “For what?”

    Sam doesn’t answer, already out of the booth and headed to re-attach to her new acquaintance. Kara gets called to the dance floor a few songs later but doesn’t manage to drag Lena with her, and Lena is left alone in the booth with Lucy for the duration of the Backstreet Boys remix.

    “I’m glad you two could make it this week,” Lucy says, leaning over to talk over the music. “We missed you both last time.” Lena pulls her eyes away from Kara doing what looks like long-memorized choreography from the music video, glancing at Lucy with a frown.

    “She wasn’t here last week? She said she was going out with you guys.”

    Lucy shakes her head. “Nope. Said she was too tired.”

    “She invited me out but I couldn’t go,” Lena explains, looking back to Kara. Kara is laughing with Sam and Winn, her smile bright enough to light up the whole bar, and like she can feel Lena looking Kara’s eyes flick back to her. Lena’s chest gets tight. “I assumed she’d still…why would she not come?”

    “I figured that now that you two are dating, she didn’t want to come without you,” Lucy says, squinting slightly.

    “Oh. Right. Yes. That makes sense, because that’s…that’s what couples do,” Lena says, wincing at her own stilted delivery. Kara is holding her gaze, dancing even more ridiculously in an effort to entice Lena onto the dance floor, and it’s hard not to smile at her antics.

    “Uh huh,” Lucy says, her smile taking on a new dimension. “You don’t say.”

    The song ends and Kara comes bounding back to the booth, and Lucy leaves to find James with one last inscrutable wink in Lena’s direction.

    Sam spends much of the later part of the night speaking intently with Kelly, and more than once Lena catches them looking over at herself and Kara with matching expressions. Lena isn’t sure exactly how to label them besides perhaps mischievous, and Sam shows no desire to leave when Lena has finally decided she’s had enough social time. She stays behind while Lena ducks out early with Kara, and by midnight she and Kara are sitting cross-legged on her hotel room floor with a scrabble board on the coffee table between them.

    “So,” Lena says carefully after Kara has shown no indication of mentioning the elephant in the room, laying down a few letters and marking down her 15 points, “partner swap week.”

    Kara’s body language shifts completely. She seems to sag, her head hanging low and her face screwing up with discomfort.

    “Ugh. Don’t remind me.”

    “We’re both going to be rudely reminded tomorrow when we end up in separate rehearsal studios,” Lena says, and Kara’s uncomfortable expression shifts into guilt.

    “I know. I should have reminded you, I just…didn’t want to think about it,” Kara admits. She seems almost as despondent about it as Lena feels, and as much as she’s terrified about what’s going to happen next week, there’s something soothing about knowing Kara doesn’t want to switch partners either.

    “I knew the schedule, it’s not your fault I was absent-minded. When will we know who our new partners are?” Lena asks, shuffling her tiles around. Kara has stopped looking at her own, focused on Lena instead.

    “You’ll get an email in the morning,” Kara says, and when Lena points at the board she lets out a weak chuckle and puts down cows for a tiny handful of points. “We already know the assignments.”

    “Is it…bad?” Lena asks, her breath baited. Kara makes another face.

    “You’ll be fine. There aren’t really any bad options for you, the guys will all do their best. But mine is awful.”

    Lena sets down nucleus and marks her 9 points. “Are you allowed to tell me?”

    “Not technically,” Kara says, sounding like she very much wants to break that rule. Even so, Lena can guess solely based on the raincloud hanging over Kara’s head.

    “Is it Leslie?”

    Kara groans, her forehead hitting the table so hard that the scrabble tiles rattle.

    “Why? Of all the people I could have been stuck with, why her?” Kara whines, muffled by the wood surface. Lena straightens the tiles with a fingertip.

    “At least it wasn’t Andrea.”

    Kara makes a strangled sort of noise. “True. Can you imagine? I expect Leslie to stab me in the back, but Andrea would hire someone else to do it and make it look like an accident.”

    “She’s not nearly as intimidating as you seem to think,” Lena says, chuckling when Kara lifts her head again. There’s a big red mark in the middle of her forehead, but she looks happy to be putting Leslie out of her mind for now.

    “Have you seen the way she looks at me every time I’m near you?”

    “I think it’s because you’re taking up so much of my time,” Lena explains, though Kara looks seriously doubtful. “We still haven’t had that coffee I promised her.”

    “No, I’m pretty sure it’s because she’s jealous.” Kara frowns down at her letters, rubbing her chin – Lena, already knowing her next move, sits back to watch her. She’s still in her makeup from the broadcast but paired with a soft blue Henley and jeans, and there’s a tiny thoughtful crinkle forming between her eyebrows as she tries to decide what move to make.

    “We broke up in college. I’m sure she’s over me by now,” Lena says. Kara answers without looking up.

    “I think you underestimate how hard you’d be to get over.”

    Kara grabs a few tiles, looking ready to make a move before putting them back with a frown, and Lena is grateful for her distraction – it gives her a few moments to get her smile under control. It’s an unconscious reflex whenever Kara pays her a compliment however small or inconsequential, but she’d rather it stay unnoticed.

    Instead Lena turns back to the original topic of their conversation, picking viciously at her thumbnail as she admits her weakness. “Kara…every time I think about getting on that stage with someone else, I feel like I can’t breathe.”

    “Hey,” Kara says softly, abandoning her scrabble tiles and reaching across the board to take Lena’s hand. “Mike is a great dancer – he won last year. He’s probably going to be so happy to be free from Leslie for a week that he’ll give you his best routine.” Lena huffs a tiny laugh, and Kara’s expression gets a little more serious. “I might not be dancing with you, but I’m going to be right there cheering you on. And I’ll be rehearsing just down the hall. I’ll come visit whenever I can to help you out.”

    “Promise?”

    “Promise.”

    While nothing could reassure Lena completely, Kara’s confidence helps to quell the anxiety a little. Lena smiles, shaking her shoulders and moving her neck like Kara showed her a few weeks ago to loosen up her tense body. Kara, looking delighted that Lena is using her tools, mirrors Lena immediately. It feels like they’re about to start a rehearsal, and it gives Lena the sense of calm that she needs.

    When they’ve both stilled again Kara looks down at her tiles again, her face lighting up when she finds her word of choice.

    “Hey! Chutzpah. 27 points!”

    Kara sets it down proudly, her lopsided grin getting a little cocky, and Lena marks the points down with an affectionate chuckle.

    “Well played. Still 32 points behind, though.”

    “Eh, that’s okay,” Kara says, grabbing a few new tiles from the bag. “I don’t mind losing. I just like hanging out with you.”

    Lena can’t tamp down her smile this time. It stays while she changes her original strategy and instead lays down a different word - likewise.

    It loses her 9 points, but Kara’s grin makes it worth it.

    They get through two games before Kara goes home. Kara doesn’t fight her when she calls a driver this time, and Lena crawls into bed around 2am with a warm glow in her chest. One rooted in nothing more than Kara’s presence. In the quiet darkness of her hotel room after a night of comfort and affirmation, it’s harder to keep up the shield she’s had around her emotions all week.

    She has feelings for Kara. And not small, ignorable ones - they're far bigger than Lena ever accounted for. Attraction, affection, friendship, longing; whatever they are, they get worse every day. And she’s just going to have to be okay with that. This situation has an expiration date – ideally it's several weeks down the line, but still. A solid, immovable end point.

    Beyond that, Lena isn’t even close to equipped to handle a relationship, let alone one that needs to span such a long distance. The idea of it, of opening herself up again and inviting the kind of pain she went through with Andrea, fills her with dread. And Kara has given no real indication of wanting a relationship, anyways.

    Maybe she needs to take Sam’s advice from weeks ago; between now and then, she can simply let herself enjoy being near Kara in whatever way their situation allows. Kara is one of the most caring, genuinely wonderful people she’s ever met. She deserves someone who can give her the same kind of free, unrestrained affection she provides so easily. Someone who isn’t terrified of the very idea of emotional intimacy. Someone who isn’t so irrevocably scarred and hardened by heartbreak that she can barely maintain a friendship. Anything Lena could provide beyond what they have right now will surely end in Kara getting hurt. Lena will do whatever it takes to keep that from happening. Even if it means burying her own feelings.

    Thankfully she has a lifetime of experience in doing so.

    The bedside table lights up with an incoming text message, and Lena squints at the screen to see a text from Sam.

    I lobe this cvity!!!1!

    So do I, Lena thinks, setting the phone aside and pulling a pillow to her chest. Who’d have thought.

    Chapter End Notes

    Partner swap week, huh? Be a shame if that caused any………drama

    The dance I vaguely based this on is this one which I know is from SYTYCD but it left a permanent impact on me: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Fs9elnS2-rM

    Chapter 6

    Chapter Notes

    BEEP BEEP, DRAMA AHEAD

    In the morning Lena gets official word that her partner for the week is Mike, and it takes every ounce of willpower she has not to just pull the duvet over her head and ignore the email. The only thing that rouses her is a text from Kara with a picture of her usual tea order, and even so it’s another 45 minutes before Lena manages to throw on the first clean set of clothes she can find and get in the car.

    She ends up being a few minutes late, but as Mike is apparently running even later she’s left waiting outside the rehearsal room door for him anyways after Kara has dropped off her tea and wished her good luck. Leslie stands impatiently down the hall, tapping her foot while she waits for Kara to unlock the door and let her into their studio, and even though all the rooms are completely identical on the inside Lena already feels strange not being in there with Kara. When the door closes softly behind Kara and Leslie, it feels more like it’s been slammed in her face.

    “Lena?”

    Andrea’s room with her new partner, Winn, is apparently right next to Mike’s. When Lena turns towards her voice she schools her features into a calm mask - Andrea is standing half in the doorway, seemingly having watched their interaction with same neutral expression on her face that she wore for most of yesterday night during the taping, and it makes her words that much more surprising.

    “I hope you know what you’re doing.”

    “I always do,” Lena says carefully. Andrea steps out of the doorway, lowering her voice and moving closer.

    “A situation like yours with Kara can implode very easily. And it leaves behind a mess for everyone involved.”

    “I’m quite capable of dealing with it myself. If we happen to break up, we’ll handle it like adults,” Lena intones, glancing at the stairwell. There’s no trace of Mike yet, and Andrea shows no sign of the conversation being over. There’s no easy escape this time.

    “I don’t mean a breakup. I mean the fallout of people finding out your relationship isn’t real.”

    Lena’s stomach plummets. She snaps her head around to Andrea again, a cold hand squeezing at her ribcage, and it’s only by the grace of a lifetime of masking her emotions that she manages to speak without her voice shaking.

    “Excuse me?”

    “I work in the media, Lena,” Andrea says, low and urgent. She looks completely serious, like she’s offering an escape to a trapped animal, and for some reason it makes Lena almost as angry as she is terrified. “I can smell a showmance a mile away, and they’re very rarely a good idea.”

    “That’s a pretty big accusation. I thought your network handled real news and not tabloid fodder?” Lena says, hoping that the indignation in her voice covers up the panic that runs beneath it. Andrea is so sure, so absolutely confident in her reading of the situation, and the last thing Lena needs is for her to know just how right she is.

    Andrea sighs. She’s still so beautiful, a face Lena still knows almost as well as she knows her own, but seeing her doesn’t instill the pain that it used to. She’s not sure if it’s simply the dullness of time or some kind of confidence Kara has gifted her, but looking at Andrea no longer feels like looking into the sun. She can see the cracks in her façade now, the stiff way she holds herself and the uneven set of her jaw. It’s the same way Andrea used to act when they were teenagers, and she was jealous over some boy passing Lena notes in class.

    Maybe Kara was right, after all.

    Whatever her motivations, Andrea’s doubt is dangerous. And Lena knows enough about her stubbornness in pursuit of a story to know that she shouldn’t be underestimated.

    “You haven’t actually denied it yet,” Andrea points out, and Lena scoffs, willing her wild pulse to slow down.

    “Because it’s absurd. And it really isn’t any of your business.”

    “I know you need the good publicity right now, but I don’t want you to get shattered if it turns out she’s just using you to climb up the ladder,” Andrea says, and something about that – her words, and the tone of friendly concern she says them in – is what finally triggers Lena’s temper.

    “I think you’re the last person who should be talking about using me for career advancement,” Lena says coldly.

    Footsteps echo up the stairwell, and Lena has never been more relieved to see a man in her life than she is when Mike appears breathless in the hallway.

    “Lena, hey! Sorry I’m late, traffic was crazy,” Mike says, fiddling with his keys in the lock and completely blind to the tension he’s interrupted. “Kara has the right idea with the bike. Please don’t tell her I left you waiting out here for 15 minutes.”

    When he’s finally managed to open the door Lena moves to follow him inside, but Andrea catches her sleeve.

    “Lee, please,” Andrea murmurs, more reserved now. “I’m only saying this because despite what you might think, I still care about you -”

    “Don’t call me that,” Lena snaps, pulling her sleeve from Andrea’s loose grip. She might finally be moving past the intensity of her hurt, but the old nickname still stings. “I don’t need your advice, nor did I ask for it. And I think you should examine your own intentions before you attack Kara’s.”

    It’s a visible blow. Andrea’s face tightens and she nods curtly, putting her hands up in retreat as she takes a step back. She knows the gravity of Lena losing her cool, especially in public, and the space between them lets Lena breathe a little easier.

    “I don’t think getting coffee is a good idea after all,” Lena finishes, crossing her arms tightly and following Mike. She’s not sure if Andrea tries to protest – Lena closes the door behind her before she can try, leaning wearily against it as the fight leaves her body all at once.

    “Uh…everything okay?” Mike asks, looking a little bit afraid of what the answer might be. “That sounded kinda intense.”

    Everything is not okay, but there isn’t much she can do about it right now. All she wants is to talk to Kara, and instead she’s stuck here with a very nervous-looking man she hardly knows.

    “Fine,” Lena says shortly, rolling her sleeves up but resolutely keeping her sweater on. “Let’s just get to work.”

    The rehearsal does not go well.

    Mike does his best, to his credit. He tries several different ways to get Lena to relax and grasp the choreography, and his assistant Gayle is just as kind as Imra always was when Lena fails. The Foxtrot they’re doing is even a fairly simple dance, steps-wise, with only a single lift and a few basic spins - it shouldn’t be hard for Lena to pick up after the increasingly complicated numbers she’s been doing with Kara over the last 4 weeks. But the simplicity of the moves means that the routine relies much more heavily on theatricality and performance to keep it interesting, and Mike is no Kara. He’s sweet and accommodating and a little goofy in an excitable Labrador sort of way, but he lacks Kara’s charm and Lena is too anxious from her conversation with Andrea to be able to plaster on a showbiz smile for him.

    The whole thing is both a terrible reminder of how it felt to struggle along in her mother’s mandated dance classes as a child, and a horrifying glimpse of what her first week might have been like if she hadn’t gotten Kara as a partner. When they stop for lunch Mike looks about as relieved as Lena feels to take a break from the mess.

    “I’m really sorry, Lena,” Mike says once he’s turned the music off, his usual carefree manner clouded. He leans heavily on the table, and Lena takes a long swig of water.

    “Why would you be sorry?”

    “You’re used to dancing with Kara. It’s pretty clear that I’m not measuring up.”

    “It has nothing to do with you,” Lena says, pushing away the bout of nerves that accompanies the reminder of Kara’s absence. “All you’re obligated to do is teach me the choreography.”

    “But you’re not just another competitor, you’re Kara’s girlfriend.”

    The words Kara’s girlfriend make her complicated feelings even more tumultuous, but she’d rather not let Mike see that.

    “Sure,” Lena says evenly, rubbing her hands together. “But I shouldn’t get special treatment for that. You’re doing an admirable job, it’s me who can’t make it work.”

    Mike doesn’t seem to know what to say to that, likely because he’s trying valiantly not to admit that that it’s true. He’s saved from needing to make something up by the door opening and Kara poking her head in.

    “Hey! Lunch?” Kara says, ignoring Mike completely. Lena is halfway to the door before she’s finished the question. They grab gyros from another one of Kara’s little hidden gem restaurants, and once they’ve settled in at an outdoor table Lena manages to get past her anxious nausea and take a few bites.

    “How’s Leslie?” Lena asks, following Kara’s lead and sprinkling some kind of seasoning mix onto her wrap before she takes another bite. Kara answers with her mouth full.

    “Please don’t make me talk about her. I need one hour where I can pretend she doesn’t exist,” Kara mumbles. “Tell me about your number – is Mike walking you through it?”

    “He’s trying,” Lena says, trying for neutrality. Kara swallows, making a sympathetic face.

    “It’s just one week. One performance, and then we’re dancing together again.”

    “If I don’t get us eliminated,” Lena scoffs. She fusses with her wrap, her appetite waning. “I can’t emote the way he wants. And I freeze every time he tries to lift me. His cologne gives me a headache, and his –”

    His hands feel wrong, she wants to say. I miss yours. But she chews at her lip instead, taking a bigger bite of food to compensate for her silence. It’s delicious, but she finds immediately that she doesn’t want to swallow it. Kara pauses her own lunch to reach across the table and put a hand on Lena’s arm.

    “Mike is a little clueless, but he means well. Maybe if I talk to him?”

    “It’s not about him,” Lena sighs, abandoning her attempt at eating entirely and crossing her arms over her chest. She stares at the colourful swirly pattern on her soda cup instead of Kara. “It would be this way with anyone. I just don’t think I can do this without you.”

    “You’re a great dancer.”

    “Only with you. Trust me, my mother had me in dance classes from a very young age, and the only reason I got to drop out and stop being mercilessly teased every week for my awkwardness was because one of my teachers took pity and admitted that she’d seen more innate rhythm in dancing bears.”

    There’s a pregnant pause after that statement, and Lena looks up from her Sprite to see Kara staring at her in abject horror.

    “Someone said that to you? As a child?” Kara says, not noticing when a large piece of tomato slides out of her gyro and onto the surface of the table. Her horror is foreign to Lena – of all the things said to and about her as a child, this doesn’t even rate in the top 100 – but Kara looks like she thinks it’s an absolute travesty.

    “Well, it was directed at my mother. But I was present at the time.”

    Kara puts her own wrap down, ignorant of the tzatziki all over her fingers. “Jeez, Lena, it’s no wonder you’re so self-conscious.”

    “It’s fine, I lived. I’ve lasted weeks longer than I ever thought I would in this competition, and the sooner I go home, the sooner you can break up with me and be free.” She does her best to make it come out like an airy joke, but Kara only looks confused and sad.

    “Why would I want that to happen sooner?”

    Kara’s expression makes Lena’s chest ache, and she crosses her arms more securely and leans forward into the edge of the table. The pressure helps. “Well for one, Andrea already suspects us.”

    “What?”

    Lena recounts her conversation with Andrea over their abandoned food – even Kara doesn’t seem interested in finishing her lunch, which feels momentous – and by the time she’s finished, their break time is almost over.

    “I’m sure it’s going to be fine,” Kara says, throwing their unfinished wraps into the trash as they head back to the studio. “She’s just one person with an opinion. I don’t think anyone would believe her.”

    “One person who works for the media,” Lena adds. Kara makes a face.

    “Right. Well, she’s under an NDA at least until the show ends, just like the rest of us.” It’s not the most comforting reassurance, and Kara frowns. “Should we say something to her? Confront her together?”

    “No,” Lena says immediately. She knows exactly what would happen if they confronted Andrea – she’d see through them in an instant, smell blood in the water and dig her heels in. “No, it would just confirm her suspicions. We’re just going to have to be more careful. More convincing. So we don’t give her any reason to doubt.”

    “Okay,” Kara says easily, reaching down to take Lena’s hand. She brings it to her mouth, pressing a kiss to the back of it before tucking it into the crook of her arm. “Easy.”

    It’s a chaste gesture, almost gentlemanly and probably meant for any unseen smartphones pointed their way to capture. But still, Lena’s heart races.

     

     

    Rehearsals with Mike don’t get much better. Mike is apologetic to an almost comical degree, constantly asking forgiveness for not being able to help her like Kara can, and Kara herself takes to coming into their studio whenever Leslie goes to the bathroom or takes a break. Her presence is comforting, but every time Lena feels like she might be starting to help Leslie will appear in the doorway like a platinum blonde specter.

    “Just give us a second, Leslie,” Kara finally snaps after a day or so of this pattern, her voice getting sharp and impatient in a way Lena hasn’t heard before. Lena is used to her endless patience, her sunny disposition – and Leslie seems delighted at being the one to finally make her crack.

    “Are you going to stop checking in on Luthor every 5 minutes, or should I let the producers know your new relationship is getting in the way of your work?” Leslie says in a jaunty tone, and Lena can see Kara react visibly. Her jaw clenches, her nostrils flare, and one of her fists curls into a tight ball.

    “It’s okay,” Lena says lowly, swallowing her usual nerves and giving Kara a quick peck on the cheek. It seems to calm her down a little. “I’ll see you after rehearsal. We’ll do something together tonight.”

    Kara takes a big, deep breath, and once the tight line of her shoulders has relaxed she nods. “Yeah. Okay.” She pulls Lena into a brief hug, and Lena takes advantage of the closeness to press in and whisper in her ear.

    “Don’t let her get to you too much. She isn’t worth it.”

    “No promises,” Kara mutters back. Her smile when she pulls away is genuine, though, and even though they’ve spent almost every evening together lately the promise of time together later is enough to get Lena through the rest of the day.

    Later finds them in Lena’s room with a bed full of snacks and the TV playing the Wizard of Oz at a low volume. Kara is cozied up next to her, her hair piled into a messy bun and the fluffy blanket covering them both, and Lena doesn’t really care about getting crumbs in her sheets while Kara munches on Pringles as long as she gets to stay in this warm cocoon. Lena wouldn’t classify it as cuddling, exactly, but they’re definitely touching more than is probably necessary in a California King.

    “I hate her.” Kara is throwing a stress ball into the air with her non-Pringle hand and catching it again as the flying monkeys attack Dorothy, morose even when her favourite movie is playing.

    “The Wicked Witch?”

    “No, Leslie.”

    “I don’t see much of a difference.”

    “Ha!” Kara snorts, leaning her head against Lena’s shoulder for a moment while she giggles. “The Wicked Witch at least had a reason to be a jerk. Dorothy did land a house on her sister.”

    Kara lifts her head again to keep throwing her stress ball, and Lena swallows her disappointment. She could easily initiate contact herself, she’s been getting quite good at it, but it feels so much easier when Kara does it. “So you hate Leslie?”

    “Yes!” Kara exclaims, squeezing the ball tightly before throwing it again. “And I don’t like hating people! I try to find redeemable qualities in everyone, but Leslie is just so…so…”

    “So what?”

    “Mean,” Kara finally says, pausing in her ball-throwing to frown thoughtfully. “That sounds juvenile, but she’s just awful to be around, you know? She never has a single nice thing to say.”

    “She seems bitter about something, but I can’t figure out what,” Lena agrees, drawing her eyes away from the movie to look at Kara. The details of her face are almost blurred in the low lamplight, but there’s something intimate in the way she so casually presses into Lena’s side. “I’d barely even heard of her before this. I’m used to being hated, but usually they tell me the reason before they shout their abuse.”

    Lena turns back to the television, but even so she can feel the weight of Kara’s eyes on the side of her face. She’s sure Kara looks sad – she always does when Lena mentions the level of vitriol she’s gotten in the past – and Kara scootches even closer.

    “She’s definitely mad that we’ve been doing so well, but it seems deeper than that,” Kara says, twisting her body until she’s on her back with her head pressed into Lena’s side. She probably can’t even see the TV, but she doesn’t seem to mind – she just starts throwing the ball again. “I dunno. What’s more frustrating is she seems to be putting in a ton of effort this week. She insults me every 15 seconds, but she’s got a fire under her butt about something.”

    “Probably trying to perform well enough to get me eliminated.”

    “You’re not going to be eliminated,” Kara says, like a reflex. Lena disagrees – if she and Mike bomb, it won’t matter how well Kara dances. The focus for swap week is on the stars and not the professionals; Mike will still get to stay and dance with Leslie for another week, and she and Kara will be out of the running.

    It sounds like Kara is trying to reassure herself as much as she is Lena, and Lena switches subjects as Kara throws the ball too hard – it bounces off the ceiling and Kara misses the catch, hitting herself squarely in the face.
