Lena laughs at the stunned expression on Kara’s face, but ball is so small and light that Kara just grabs it again and tosses it a little lower.

    “Where did you even get that thing?” Lena asks, her eyes following the rise and fall of the little blue foam sphere. Kara shrugs.

    “I signed up for an email list. One of those street canvassers.”

    “You signed up for spam emails just to get a free ball?”

    “Well technically it was Keira Darkwing who signed up,” Kara says, grinning. “She loves free stuff, and she’s very invested in…” Kara checks the ball, reading off the company name, “Rippin’ Rod’s Fitness Supplements.”

    “Keira Darkwing,” Lena drawls.

    “Uh huh.”

    “What is she, Batman’s ward?”

    Kara laughs, missing her catch again. “I could have used Kora Danger, she’s a secret agent on the lookout for the best deals in auto insurance. Last week she got me a free koozie cup and a bunch of pens.”

    “How many fake identities do you have, exactly?” Lena asks, grabbing the ball holding it hostage for a moment. She squeezes it a few times before tossing it gently at Kara’s head – it bounces off her forehead, and Kara grabs for it with a grin.

    “I’m a woman of many sides. And I hate giving out my real email.”

    Across the room the credits start to roll, and Kara grabs for the remote to switch from DVD to streaming services for their next film.

    “We don’t have to keep going,” Lena says, glancing at the clock – it’s 11pm already, and getting up in the morning for rehearsals has been harder when she isn’t spending the day with Kara. “It’s already late, and mine is a long movie.” But Kara selects the film anyways, settling back into the pillows.

    “Hey, don’t deprive me of the opportunity to experience you watching Star Wars. I’ve been looking forward to this all week!”

    The opening crawl starts, the theme music blasting over yellow text, and a sense of calm washes over Lena. She’d scoffed a little at the idea when Kara had asked what her comfort movie was, but now that it’s on she can’t deny that that’s exactly what it is.

    “So, why Star Wars?” Kara asks, her eyes wandering to Lena rather than the movie. Lena keeps hers glued to the screen.

    “I already told you. I liked space and Carrie Fisher.”

    “Nice and simple?” Kara grins. Lena rolls her eyes.

    “I used to watch it with Lex,” she elaborates, shifting a little so that more of her is pressed into Kara without seeming obvious. “When we were kids, before he considered himself above it all.”

    “You two were close, huh?”

    After years of press hounding, Lena has developed a hard shell when it comes to questions about her brother. But Kara is asking with such genuine interest, such empathy, that Lena is talking before she really thinks about the words.

    “Very. He was the only person in that house who didn’t make me feel like an inconvenience. For a long time, he…he felt like home.” Lena clears her throat, swallowing past the lump that’s materialized there. “Until he didn’t. I haven’t really had that feeling since.”

    She doesn’t dare look at Kara for fear of losing her tenuous grip on not crying, but Kara’s compassion still comes through in her voice. “I’m glad you can still enjoy it without him.”

    Only with you, Lena thinks, biting hard at the inside of her cheek. It helps ground her, and she’s able to actually look at Kara when she turns the tables.

    “What about you? Why the Wizard of Oz?”

    “Glinda’s pretty hot,” Kara says nonchalantly. Lena snorts, elbowing her in the side.

    “Be serious.”

    “I am! I’m the most serious, always, and I resent that accusation.”

    “You’re a dork is what you are,” Lena says, and even she can hear the affection in her own voice.

    Kara pulls the blanket fully over both of them without elaborating, nestling until she’s created a perfect indent in the pillows, and mindlessly Lena settles too. She leans her head into Kara’s shoulder, and in conditions like that it’s impossible to feel anything but safe and comfortable – Kara is so warm and she smells nice, not like any one thing Lena can identify but a soothing combination that feels unique. She tries her best to stay awake, but as the droids land on Tattooine Lena closes her eyes for just a moment only to open them to the weak light of morning shining through the white curtains.

    She slept straight through the night, and Kara never left.

    Kara must have fallen asleep soon after she did. They’re pressed even closer now, their feet slightly entangled, and Kara’s fingers are caught up in the ends of Lena’s hair almost like she’d been playing with it when she fell asleep. Her face is smooth and calm, her mouth slightly open as she snores lightly, and when Kara mumbles a little in her sleep and swings an arm over Lena’s middle to pull her closer it drives the reality of this week home – there’s a good chance that after Monday, Lena is going to be leaving.

    It's not even her nerves talking, in this sleepy state. It’s an absolute sureness. Her routine with Mike isn’t going to get much better. There’s a high probability that without Kara she’s going to be sent home, and the idea of this all ending is exponentially worse than she imagined. It’s worse than the dread she felt about coming here in the first place. They haven’t even performed, and already Lena is mourning something that isn’t lost yet.

    Kara’s breathing shifts, and Lena doesn’t have time to move out of her space or pretend she wasn’t just watching her sleep before Kara frowns a little and blinks her eyes open. Her gaze is sleepy and unfocused, but she doesn’t seem shocked at all to have woken up with her face inches from Lena’s and she doesn’t move away. Lena doesn’t want her to, either; she could even swear, for a brief moment, that Kara moves closer. Close enough that Lena can anticipate the sensation of their noses brushing.

    An alarm goes off on Kara’s phone, blaring a jaunty melody from Kara’s pocket so loudly that it vibrates the mattress, and that more than anything wakes Lena up. Kara startles, staring at Lena blankly at first before glancing around the room with a look of mild panic, and once she’s processed where she is she shuts the alarm off and rubs her eyes with a light chuckle.

    “Wow, I do not remember falling asleep.”

    “Neither do I,” Lena says, keeping her voice quiet. Speaking at a normal volume feels too real for this strange little moment they’re in. And Lena is suddenly hyper-aware of her own possible morning breath.

    “Sorry, I totally crashed without asking,” Kara says, her voice going a little high and squeaky as she stretches out like a cat and pops various joints. “I would have at least slept on the couch.”

    “Don’t worry about it,” Lena assures her, averting her eyes when Kara’s stretching pulls her shirt up a little. “This bed is huge.”

    Kara seems to realize then just how close they are, and as she finishes her stretch she disentangles their legs and moves away. It leaves Lena feeling cold as Kara gets up and heads to the bathroom, so she burrows a little deeper into the duvet.

    “At least it’ll look good, right?” Lena muses, smoothing her hand over the warm spot Kara was just occupying. “You leaving my hotel in the wee hours of the morning.”

    “Yeah, I guess so,” Kara calls from the bathroom, her voice raising over the running of the sink. “Ugh. I need a shower.”

    “Do you have time to get home and have one before rehearsal starts?”

    Kara emerges from the bathroom drying her wet hands on her sweatpants. “If I bike super fast, maybe. Biking fast sort of negates the shower though.”

    “Sorry,” Lena winces. “You’re free to use mine, but you’d have to put your same clothes on again after.”

    “Maybe next time. Leslie will just have to deal with my stink today,” Kara says with a wink, sitting down on the edge of the bed to tie her shoes. “I’ll see you for lunch, right?”

    Kara leaves with a promise to actually watch all of Star Wars next time, and after she leaves Lena can’t seem to get back to sleep. She lies awake in the dim light, hugging Kara’s pillow to her chest and staring at the ceiling until her alarm goes off and she hauls herself out of bed.

    If Lena notices Kara leaving things at her hotel room in the days after to make accidental sleepovers easier, she doesn’t say a word.

     

     

    The routine doesn’t get much better as the week progresses. Lena knows the steps, can execute the choreography without making any mistakes, but she can’t make herself perform the acting part of it like she knows she’s supposed to. With Kara it feels effortless; it comes out of her without trying, flowing like water just through Kara’s proximity. With Mike, no matter how helpful he tries to be it feels like whatever emotion she’s supposed to be accessing is on the other side of an Old Spice-scented wall.

    After Leslie’s comment she and Kara are more careful only to see each other at lunch, and it’s a relief when the cast decides to go out for karaoke on Friday night. Lena isn’t usually one to need to blow off steam but Mike and Kara both clearly need it, and dealing with Leslie all week is enough to drive even Lena to drink a bit.

    Even so, she still refuses to sing.

    Again Lena finds herself in the increasingly comfortable position of watching from a booth while Kara lets loose with her friends, and she pours another round for everyone from the communal pitcher of beer when Sara and Imra flop back into the booth, breathless from their duet. 

    Sara is quickly distracted by a loud conversation with Mike about motorcycles, but Imra scootches closer to Lena with a tipsy smile.

    “How’s your week with Mike? Is he giving you trouble? I’m happy to punish him for you.”

    Lena snorts, taking a long sip of lager. “He’s fine. My issues are my own fault.”

    Lena’s eyes find Kara again, and she can’t stop herself from smiling while the blonde does a spirited all-out performance of an upbeat pop song Lena doesn’t know. She looks to be having a great time, and Imra follows Lena’s eyeline to where Kara is being lifted up by Winn and Alex to yell the song’s rap interlude from above.

    Lena shouldn’t be surprised by now that Kara is good at everything, but she really does have a staggeringly beautiful singing voice.

    “Yeah. She’s a difficult partner to give up, isn’t she?” Imra interrupts Lena’s musing, smiling kindly when Lena blushes over being caught staring. “I think most of the cast has been in love with her at some time or another.”

    “She mentioned that she’d dated a few people but none worked out,” Lena fills in, and Imra nods.

    “She’s much less emotionally available than she comes across. I’m sure you’ve noticed. She wants to help everyone, but when it comes to her own vulnerabilities she’ll do anything to change the subject. Even if it makes people drift away.”

    Imra is clearly just making conversation, but even in her tipsy haze it makes Lena think. It’s similar to what Alex had said about Kara in that terrible bathroom confrontation – that Kara is more emotionally complicated than she seems. It’s true that Kara tends to shy away from talking about her own feelings, focusing mostly on supporting Lena’s many anxieties. And Lena, in her selfishness, hadn’t really noticed. Kara has opened up enough to make Lena feel comfortable doing the same, but now that Lena thinks about it, it’s only been in snapshots – brief, lovely moments of candidness that Kara usually ends quickly with a joke or by changing the subject.

    She’s in no way entitled to Kara’s deeper feelings, but the idea that she might have unintentionally made an environment where Kara isn’t comfortable sharing makes Lena feel strange.

    “I’m sorry, really I don’t mean to insinuate myself into your relationship!” Imra is quick to say, clearly picking up on Lena’s sudden silence. “I meant it more to say that I’ve never seen Kara as happy as she is right now. You really seem to be an exception.”

    Kara’s song ends, and the blonde runs right back to the songbook to choose her next one with a quick wink in Lena’s direction. Mike and Sara interrupt soon after to entice Imra into a drinking game, but Lena excuses herself politely from the table; she isn’t sure what to do with herself, exactly, but she feels restless. Unsettled. She’s considering going outside for some fresh air when Kara jogs over, leaving the stage to Winn to sing a dramatic showtune.

    “Hey! What’s up?” Kara pants, her face flushed red from dancing and drinks. “You look like you’re about to bolt.”

    “I think I just need a little quiet,” Lena says, raising her voice to be heard over the music. Winn’s voice is good, but not nearly as engaging as Kara’s. “I might go home.”

    “I’ll go with you!” Kara is already heading to the door, but Lena catches her elbow.

    “No, you should stay. You’re still having a good time; I don’t want to be the buzzkill.”

    “Nah, I hang out with these guys all the time!” Kara says with a careless wave, using Lena’s grip on her arm to tug her gently towards the exit. “I’d rather stay with you, if that’s okay. Come on, we’ll get a cab.”

    Lena is quiet for the first few minutes of the cab ride, mulling over Imra’s words and staring out the window at the city passing by, and she can feel Kara glancing over at her every few seconds until finally the blonde breaks.

    “Are you okay, Lena?”

    Kara’s voice is small and unsure, likely assuming she’s somehow the source of Lena’s melancholy, and Lena can’t bring herself to lie.

    “Yes,” Lena says, gathering her thoughts and giving Kara a small encouraging smile. “I’m fine. Just something Imra said.”

    “What did she say?” Kara asks immediately, frowning, but Lena deflects.

    “Nothing important. It just made me realize how much emotional real estate I’ve been taking up in our friendship.”

    Just as Lena suspected she might, Kara automatically comes to Lena’s defense even against her own observation. She turns fully towards Lena and gives her the full weight of her attention, and under the spotlight of her concern Lena almost wishes she hadn’t said anything.

    “What are you talking about? You don’t take up real estate. Our relationship isn’t a property deal.”

    “You consistently go out of your way to help me through all my issues,” Lena says reluctantly, her fingers twisting together in her lap. “I suppose I’m just worried that I’ve been taking up all the space. Not leaving any room for you.”

    Kara doesn’t say anything for a time. The silence in the cab isn’t overly comfortable, but it isn’t deafening either – Kara just looks like she’s thinking something over, her teeth worrying at her lower lip, and in the end whatever decision she makes is a mystery to Lena.

    “Do you want to go to my place instead?” Kara says. It’s entirely without context, and Lena hesitates; her instinctive answer is an unequivocal yes, but they’ve avoided Kara’s apartment up until this point for a reason.

    “I thought we agreed not to? I don’t want the press hounding you there.”

    “I think we can risk it one time.” There’s no reluctance in Kara’s voice, just her usual gentle optimism. It’s as persuasive as usual.

    “If you’re sure?” Lena says, and when Kara nods enthusiastically Lena’s mind is made for her. “Then I’d love to, actually. I’m getting a bit tired of being in my hotel room all the time.”

    Kara redirects the cab. Kara’s place is much closer to the karaoke bar than Lena’s hotel, so in just a few minutes Kara is leading her up the stairwell and into her cozy little apartment. Kara buzzes around the space at first, plugging in fairy lights and lighting a candle until the whole space is lit with a warm yellow glow, and while Kara makes them hot chocolate Lena gets her first real look around. The last time she was here they spent all their time on the roof, and Lena had only really been able to take in the basics – this time she memorizes the details, all the tiny indications of Kara’s habits. Kara’s clothes are all stored on a rack near her half-made bed, her bedside table stacked high with books and half-empty cups of water. The whole space is littered with throw blankets and knickknacks, and the walls are hung with a variety of framed paintings.

    “This is lovely,” Lena remarks, drifting closer to a particularly beautiful piece in the living room. It’s almost abstract, but Lena can see the meaning in the strokes of it – like a streaky city skyline, the background all reds and sunset-oranges. A halo of fire.

    “Thank you!” Kara says from the kitchen, vigorously whisking something in a copper pot. “That one’s mine.”

    Lena turns back towards her. She looks to be making the hot chocolate from scratch, and her focus on the process is singular. “You painted this?”

    “Yup! It’s nothing to write home about, but I like doing it.”

    Kara’s humility is endearing, but totally unwarranted. She’s incredible at all forms of art, it seems, and as Lena’s skills amount to not much more than stick figures and a bit of piano she’s even more impressed by Kara’s easy command of creativity.

    “Kara, it’s beautiful.”

    “Thanks.” Kara grins at her, opening up a big bag of marshmallows.

    Lena moves on to the shelves near Kara’s TV, cluttered with framed photos. She can see some with Alex and Kelly, a few with the show cast, and two with an older blonde woman with a strong similarity to Alex that Lena assumes must be Eliza. One photo stands out among the rest, though – it’s old, faded and a little washed-out, but even at a much younger age Lena can still recognize Kara’s features. The adults on either side of her are in dark clothes, almost robe-like; Lena can definitely see a family resemblance in their faces, but they look stern and formal in a way that seems contrary to what she knows Kara to be.

    “My parents,” Kara confirms, appearing at Lena’s elbow and handing her a steaming mug. “On my 12th birthday.”

    “It looks a little serious for a birthday party,” Lena remarks. Kara shrugs, taking a sip of her own drink. It gives her a marshmallow mustache, and Lena almost raises a hand to wipe it off for her before she catches herself – instead she just points at it, and Kara wipes it away.

    “It wasn’t really a party. More of a ceremonial coming of age,” Kara says, more concerned with getting the marshmallow off her hand. Lena chuckles.

    “Yikes. Sounds sort of cult-y.”

    Kara is silent. Her only indication that she’s bothered by Lena’s comment is a brief pause in her movement, a flicker across her face, but it’s enough for Lena’s stomach to drop to her feet. It’s obvious that accidentally, idiotically, Lena has struck a nerve.

    “Oh. Oh, Kara, I’m sorry.” Lena is torn between wanting to put a hand on Kara’s arm, and being sure that Kara won’t welcome her touch after she so eloquently put her foot in her mouth. “I shouldn’t have – that was a stupid thing to joke about.”

    “No, it’s fine. Most people don’t grow up in communes, I’ve learned not to be too sensitive about it,” Kara says, but there’s something different behind her usual easygoing smile. Her eyes don’t crinkle in quite the same way. Lena has no idea what to say, no idea how to even begin to make this better, so instead she tries to do what Kara always does for her. She tries to hold space. Kara sits heavily on her well-worn couch, and she doesn’t move away when Lena settles in next to her. In fact she leans into her, tucking her legs up so that her feet sit just under Lena’s thigh.

    “When I turned twelve I was supposed to start being a real part of it all, but they sent me away instead,” Kara elaborates after a bit, picking at a loose string near the ankle of her pants. “I think they were trying to protect me, but…at the time it just felt like I was being abandoned. They died pretty soon afterwards. The whole commune did. It was reported as some kind of accident, but there was something hinky about it.”

    Lena hardly moves. She sits stock-still, clutching her hot chocolate without drinking it, as if moving might somehow break the moment. She knows what it’s like to bare parts of yourself, even knowing that the other person will be kind; she’s done it with Kara more than once. She’s just entirely unused to being the receiving party. When she speaks, she keeps her voice low.

    “I can’t even imagine what that was like.”

    “Pretty weird,” Kara says with a melancholy smile. “I thought it was all totally normal until Clark looked into it. He never found out what happened though. His parents were involved too, but he was a lot younger at the time.” Kara’s fidgeting turns into unravelling most of the stitches at the bottom of her jeans, and she twists the strings around her fingers until they turn white. “I grew up in such an insular way that usually it feels safer to not share things.”

    It’s a side of Kara she’s never seen before, quiet and nervous and not quite able to look Lena in the eye, and she takes a few moments before responding. It does explain a lot about Kara – her persistent sunniness, her avoidance of vulnerability, her focus on the comfort of others before herself. It’s all part of who she is, definitely, but it also functions as a mask every bit as much as Lena’s ice queen persona. A protective barrier, presenting the more palatable parts of herself to keep the deeper parts out of the public eye.

    “I understand that. Probably more than most,” Lena finally says, setting her mug down on the coffee table. “But I want you to know that you can lean on me. You’ve made me feel safe here, and I hope I can be that for you too. If you want that.”

    “It’s not because you take up space,” Kara insists immediately, putting a hand – warm from holding the mug of cocoa, and so soft – over Lena’s. “I need you to know that. I just don’t open up very much. My past is weird, and I…I’m always scared that if I talk about my real feelings, the difficult ones, people will leave. That they’ll think I’m not worth the effort. It’s easier to keep things light. Means I’m never a burden.”

    Kara ends with another of those melancholy smiles, and Lena squeezes the hand she was offered.

    “Nobody in their right mind would think you’re a burden.”

    Kara doesn’t address that. She turns more fully towards Lena instead, looking her right in the eyes and seeming determined to finish this out. “I do trust you, Lena. Really.”

    Lena, caught in the tractor beam of Kara’s earnestness, swallows down the near-overwhelming urge to curl into a ball. Until now she’s been focused on Kara, but now that Kara is back in her usual comfort zone of making Lena feel worthy with only a few devastatingly-placed words she’s reminded that her mother would have called this honesty weakness. Instead she nods, even managing to speak without her voice cracking. “I’m glad.”

    “I mean if there’s anyone who can relate to my weird family stuff it has to be you, right?”

    Lena laughs, startling even herself, and the tension she hadn’t realized was building in the room breaks a little when Kara grins back. “Now I see why you were so understanding.”

    “Yeah. And, um,” Kara pulls her bottom lip through her teeth, her eyes averting briefly back down to the couch again. “You asked about the Wizard of Oz? I liked it because…”

    Kara falters. She looks like she’s struggling to find the words, and Lena squeezes her hand again.

    “It’s okay. I think I understand.”

    Kara’s relief is palpable. She sags back into the couch, exhaling in a big whoosh and wiping at her eyes; Lena can see the wall going back up to an extent, the nonchalant and easygoing Kara making a reappearance, but it’s not nearly as solid as it was before. Kara seems more relaxed, a little softer at the edges; less like she’s putting on a mask, and more like Kara is just intentionally shifting moods.

    “Okay. Cool,” Kara says, picking up her cocoa again and taking a big mouthful. “Now that I’ve totally brought down the energy of the night, I just have one question for you.”

    “You didn’t, but what’s your question?”

    “Can we watch Star Wars now?”

    They watch the whole movie curled up on the couch, and even though Lena doesn’t end up getting back to the hotel until well past 3am and it definitely shows in rehearsal the next day she can’t bring herself to regret it. Not when she’s so sure that her time with Kara is soon going to be cut short.

     

     

    Rehearsals pick up over the weekend, usually going late and replacing her time with Kara in an effort to just get Lena to the bare minimum with the choreography, and when filming day finally comes she feels like a wrung-out sponge. She’s not nervous yet excited like she usually is when she’s dancing with Kara, she’s not even terrified like she was her first week – she’s just numb. An absence of feeling. She gets some small comfort when she finally sees Kara at the catering truck in the morning after two days of only talking on the phone, but they’re separated for wardrobe soon after and all Lena can do is shut herself down and hope that it’ll all be over soon.

    Leslie does her best to badger her in the hair and makeup trailer, gloating and throwing barbs, but Lena has had enough practice in shutting off her feelings while reporters scream at her that she’s able to detach herself and ignore her completely. Leslie is in a fit by the end of it, seething at not being able to get a rise out of her, but Lena leaves in an almost dissociative state.

    Reality only starts to leech back in again when she runs into Kara backstage. She almost bowls her over in fact, walking aimlessly without really seeing her surroundings, and Kara’s warm hand on her shoulder feels like a hot iron searing through a layer of ice.

    “How are you feeling?” Kara asks quietly, leading Lena to a more secluded corner and away from the chaos. Usually Lena would shrug or give Kara a wry answer, but this time she too tired to be anything but honest.

    “I don’t know.”

    Kara frowns, and Lena can only offer a small, empty smile.

    “I know it sounds strange. But I couldn’t tell you what I’m feeling right now. I don’t know.”

    “Can I hug you?” Kara asks after a brief pause, and for some reason that – the asking, and the strange tone Kara said it in – makes the numbness recede a little. She nods, and Kara pulls her into a hug that feels different.

    Kara usually hugs briefly but frequently, or scoops Lena into big crushing embraces when she’s excited. Her usual hugs are almost thoughtless, just an extension of her tactile expressions of affection. This one is firm, but calm. An even pressure on all sides of her, holding her together in a way that lets Lena relax her grip on herself and fall apart a little. It makes the noise around them fade enough for Lena to hear her own thoughts. She presses her face into Kara’s chest, feeling long-fought tears rise up enough to make her eyes water, and takes a big breath.

    “Resigned,” Lena finally says finally when she’s successfully fought the tears back down, muffled by Kara’s shirt. “That’s what I feel. Like I’m about to fail and I can’t do anything to stop it.”

    Much like Lena didn’t offer any of her usual responses, Kara doesn’t offer empty platitudes. She changes her grip a little, resting her chin on the top of Lena’s head, and hums a little. It makes her chest rumble under Lena’s cheek.

    “I can’t guarantee that everything is going to be okay. But I’m going to be here no matter what.”

    It’s infinitely more comforting than a simple everything will be fine. And it’s surprising seeing as she stopped trusting people a long time ago, but in that moment Lena actually believes her.

    When they finally part Lena catches sight of Andrea standing near the wings to the stage, staring at them with an expression she can’t decipher. Their eyes meet only briefly, and Lena presses herself more fully into Kara’s arms; a flicker of something crosses Andrea’s face and for a brief and horrifying second Lena thinks she might come over to talk to them, but they’re all called to get into position soon after.

    The show starts as usual, with the exception of Kara being all the way at the other end of the stage with Leslie. Lena is third on the docket with Mike, and after suffering through Andrea and Winn’s Swing routine and Siobhan and James doing an admittedly good Contemporary number Lena is onstage in the starting position while their painfully awkward video plays on the jumbo screen. Mike had done his best to channel Kara while the crew was filming, but it’s clear to Lena and probably everyone else that the baseline chemistry just isn’t there.

    You’re gonna do great, Mike mouths as the video finishes up, giving her his best encouraging smile probably at Kara’s behest, but it does nothing to shake Lena’s deep sense of dread. Even his most enthusiastic pep talks are miles less effective than a simple squeeze of her hand from Kara would be.

    The music starts, and for the first time since she stepped foot on this stage weeks ago Lena truly comprehends the enormity of the audience.

    She’s aware of everything in a way she’s never been before. The hot stage lights are blinding, and it makes it hard to smile in a way that’s even halfway convincing; she can feel every eye in the theatre on her, and the cameras moving around at the edges of the stage only remind her just how many more people are watching from home. The routine requires her to play to the crowd, to put on a reasonable performance of having fun, but every millisecond of it feels like an impossible burden. She’s used to feeling the beats she’s supposed to hit in the shifting muscles of Kara’s body, in the rhythm they create together as much as in the music – with Mike, she can’t find them at all. Like she’s forgotten an entire language and is having to make do by stumbling through with a phrasebook.

    She knows the steps, at the very least. She’s on-beat and she hasn’t missed any marks, which is more than could have been said about her in the first days of rehearsing her first routine with Kara. She’s even smiling, though she isn’t sure it’s not coming across as a grimace. She can’t summon the kind of vigour that the dance requires but it could be worse. It could be worse.

    Until she falls.

    It’s both of their faults, and Lena knows it even as it’s happening. She’s so used to Kara’s sure hands, her single-minded focus on always being where she needs to be, and Mike just isn’t the same. Her hand slips from Mike’s sweaty one during the first moments of the lift, and when he tries to grab it again his hand lands in the wrong spot, grabbing closer to her elbow. The unfamiliarity of the hold makes her feel unsteady, unsafe, and unlike Kara he doesn’t pick up on her discomfort and adapt on the fly. When he tries to execute the rest of the lift rather than skipping it, she flinches – curling up instead of letting him support her, and even as he tries valiantly to catch her he isn’t quite fast enough. Her momentum is too much.

    She hits the stage hard, knocking the breath out of her lungs, and even over the music and the crowd’s collective gasp she can hear Kara shout her name.

    She said in the first week that her goal was not to fall on her face. And even in that most basic goal, she’s failed. The music plays on; she doesn’t have time to lie here and let the lights spin around her head. It's humiliating being helped back to her feet by Mike to shakily finish out the rest of the number, but she does it. It’s humiliating to have an audience see it, to have failed so dramatically in front of a live broadcast and the judges who have had nothing but praise for her before. She can see their looks of concern, the shocked faces of the audience, the AV team with their hands hovering on the metaphorical cut to commercial button in case she was injured. But above all, she wishes Kara hadn’t had to see it happen. The idea of Kara being disappointed in her is too much to bear.

    After a tortuous final minute the routine ends, and Lena knows without a doubt that the applause as they approach the judging table is mostly pity.

    “I’m sorry to say it, Lena, but the fire you’ve had the last few weeks just wasn’t there today,” William says when they’ve taken their place next to Brainy, and though he sounds genuinely regretful it still stings. “You were struggling. The performance got the better of you, I think. You just weren’t up to par.”

    Jonn’s turn comes, and the lines in his face are deeper than usual.

    “It’s so difficult when you’re doing so well – it makes it so much worse when you falter. I think you know what went wrong, both of you,” he rumbles, his face sympathetic but his critique unrelenting. “Falls happen to the best of us, and you recovered as much as you could. I commend you for that. But it should have been a fun routine, and you didn’t have that trust in each other. You didn’t have the magic.”

    Cat’s disappointment is the hardest to bear of the three. Her face is a bit softer than usual, but her words still crack like a whip.

    “Just as chemistry can make a partnership sing, the lack of it can send you reeling,” Cat says heavily, and it hits Lena deep in her gut. “We all know that it only takes one bad week to take down a great run on this show. Pray that this is not your week, because that performance was not enough to keep you at the heights you’ve gotten used to.”

    Lena manages to hold it together until exactly the moment she steps backstage at the commercial break to find Kara waiting. She takes a big, shuddering breath, and immediately Kara has wrapped her up in the biggest, most comforting hug she can imagine – tight but not suffocating, one hand securely in the middle of her back and the other cradling the back of her neck under her hair. Her feet lift off the ground, and Lena clings to her like she’s a life raft in the ocean.

    “I don’t care,” Lena chants into Kara’s neck, over and over like a mantra as she fights off sobs she wasn’t expecting. “I don’t care, I don’t care –”

    “It’s okay to care,” Kara murmurs in her ear, low enough to be just for the two of them. She presses a soft kiss to the side of Lena’s head, and even though it’s over her hair the spot tingles. “It’s okay, Lena.”

    It shouldn’t feel this awful, this world-ending to have one bad routine. Everyone else has had at least one week of bad reviews if not more; she and Kara were the anomaly, until now. But nobody else has fallen on stage. And the less couples left on the show, the more likely that one bad routine will be the one to send her back to Metropolis.

    Now that it’s become a reality, the dread she’s felt all week feels even worse.

    Kara seems to be able to sense when Lena has gotten herself together. Her feet touch the ground again, and when Lena pulls away she can see that two of the makeup artists are hovering around the periphery, looking antsy about the state of her mascara.

    “You’re not supposed to be back here,” Lena says to Kara when she feels like she can talk without her voice cracking, letting one of the artists refresh her sweaty powder. “You’re up next after commercial.”

    Kara makes a face. “Did you really think I wasn’t going to come and make sure you’re okay?”

    “I’m fine,” Lena insists in flagrant disregard of the fact that she was near-weeping in Kara’s arms 30 seconds ago, but Kara is kept from replying by the grating shout of the absolute last person Lena wants to see right now.

    “Danvers!” Leslie grouses, stalking towards them and pointing back towards the stage. It’s being re-set during the commercial break, of which they have about 3 minutes left. “Quit babying your little girlfriend and get your ass back out there. We have a routine to do.”

    Kara looks ready to uppercut Leslie into next week, but this time it’s Lena who steps in front of her. Her tolerance of Leslie’s constant abuse has been wavering all week, and now that she’s already suffered the most humiliating worst case scenario Lena finds herself not caring about the consequences of saying so. She steps into Leslie’s space, shutting down her emotions in much the same way she did just after her family life imploded. Her heartbeat slows; her mind clears, and she feels cold and calm. Like putting on a pair of uncomfortable shoes she hasn’t worn in a while, but they still fit perfectly.

    “Why do you hate me so much?” Lena says, soft and calm. Leslie seems more unsettled by it than she ever was by Kara’s anger. “Is it my brother? Did his mania affect you somehow?”

    “I don’t give a fuck about your brother,” Leslie scoffs. Lena steps closer, and she’s gratified when Leslie takes a step back.

    “Then what? What is it about me that makes you humiliate yourself in public just to get a dig in? Are you really that desperate for attention?”

    Leslie’s eyes dart around. The only escape route at this point would be either to leave the are and miss her slot or go back towards the stage without Kara, and she’s unwilling to do either. Lena has caged her.

    “Did you know I asked for a female partner, too?” Leslie spits out once she’s realized that same thing, her face set in an aggression that Lena still can’t understand.

    “They didn’t tell us that,” Kara says, but Leslie only has eyes for Lena.

    “You’re not the only one, Luthor. I asked too, and they gave it to you,” Leslie snarls. Every muscle in her body seems to be prepared to fight, but Lena has no intention of getting physical. She’s never needed anything but her words. “Said it would be too much to have two of us. That if I accepted the contract, I’d have to dance with a guy just like everyone else. I guess I wasn’t the better story.”

    “Are you expecting sympathy?” Lena asks, not shy about letting her derision show. A few of the other cast members have filed in to investigate, and Lena holds firm as Mike appears beside Kara looking ready to intervene. “What about that executive decision gives you the need to attack me at every turn?”

    “You got it without even wanting to be here!” Leslie shouts, finally losing her cool entirely. Her eyes are wild, full of inexplicable rage, but Lena doesn’t flinch. “I wanted this. I’ve been working my ass off. In the first photoshoot you did nothing but bitch and insist that you didn’t care if you went home first, and yet the judges eat up every routine you drag out. You’re only still here because she wants you to be.” Leslie gestures widely at Kara, and Lena shakes her head with a scoff she’s perfected for bringing condescending businessmen down a peg or two.

    “You have no idea what I do or do not want. You know nothing about me. And the amount of effort you’ve put into hating me for something so inconsequential is quite frankly pathetic.”

    She’s never seen Leslie this agitated. Her cheeks are red, and she looks around at the watching crowd before making a twitchy sort of movement that makes Lena think for a brief moment that Leslie might take a swing at her. She sets her jaw, preparing herself for the blow, but at the last moment Leslie seems to change her mind.

    “It doesn’t matter. You’re going home,” Leslie scoffs, walking backwards towards the stage as Alex comes into view and starts to herd everyone frantically stageside. “All I had to do was hand you the noose.”

    Kara is at Lena’s side as soon as the confrontation is over. She doesn’t say anything, but there’s a sort of pride to the way she touches Lena’s waist.

    “What did she mean by that?” Mike says, staring after Leslie and looking like he’s trying to solve a complicated math problem in his head. Lena shakes it off.

    “Never mind. The break is almost over,” she says to Kara, giving her shoulder a push. Neither of them have time to get caught up in trying to decipher it right now. “You should get back out before you miss your cue.”

    Kara and Leslie don’t blow anyone away with their routine, but unfortunately Leslie manages not to fall.

    In fact she puts in more effort and passion for their Broadway routine than Lena has seen from her so far, probably rooted in pure rage, despite Kara hardly showing any emotion at all. Kara is constantly looking over to where Lena stands at the side of the stage with Mike, definitely enough to be noticeable, but Lena can’t feel much of anything as she watches it; not anger, not sadness, not even the burning shame of being trotted out with Kara, Leslie, Lucy, and Morgan Edge to stand at the back of the stage for the bottom three deliberation at the end of the show. Usually at this point she’s backstage already with Kara planning how they’re going to spend their night together. Now they’re both awaiting their fates, technically together but too far apart to seek any comfort.

    In the end, the fan votes come out to a tie.

    “It’s been quite some time since we had a tie!” Brainy says in his showman’s voice, gesturing the four of them to come forward while Kelly and Max get to escape backstage, spared for the week. “We all know what this means – a judge’s vote!”

    Lena actually has very little idea of what this means, but she can suss it out from context as she watches what happens after the announcement. All three judges are already writing something on small personalized whiteboards, and Brainy is saying something about overall scores and cumulative performance but all she can focus on is the fact that Leslie has positioned herself at Kara’s side in a way that keeps Lena from reaching for her hand.

    William is the first one to hold up his whiteboard.

    “I have to go with the better performance here tonight. Keep it simple,” William says, flipping over his board to show Leslie’s name. “My vote goes for Miss Willis to stay. I’m sorry, Lena.”

    Leslie’s face lights up with savage glee. In the corner of her eye Lena can see that Leslie is staring at her, but she doesn’t signify her with an acknowledgement. Cat is next, though, and when she flips her board Lena can see that it’s her name on it instead. Leslie’s shoulders fall, but even with this tiny bit of good news Lena can’t bring herself to hope yet.

    “Seeing as this is Lena’s first strike, I’m going with the best cumulative performance over this season,” Cat says, underlining her own neat script. “Leslie has been in the bottom a few too many times to be truly deserving of a place in the top 5. My vote goes with Miss Luthor.”

    Leslie looks ready to jump onto the judge’s table and rip out Cat’s expensive earrings, but Lena makes a mental note to get Jess to arrange a large donation to Cat Grant’s charity of choice.

    The decision comes down to Jonn. He looks grave as he stares down at his own whiteboard, his head down and his hands folded in a tight ball in front of his face, and before he turns his board over he looks up at them with a deep frown.

    “My critique has always been based in technique and nothing else. This is one of the hardest decisions I’ve ever had to make on this show, but I need to keep true to that. So based purely on my own scoring criteria,” Jonn says, slowly flipping his vote over, “Lena, I’m sorry, but your stumble puts you three points lower.”

    Leslie is scrawled across the board, and Lena’s world goes gray.

    She hardly has time to feel anything besides a terrible, blank sort of paralysis before Kara has brushed past Leslie to hug her fiercely. Kara is shaking, holding Lena far too tight but in a way that somehow feels good, but whether it’s from anger or something else she’s not sure. There’s a lot happening on stage, but as Kara lets her go and the cameras come in close for their reaction shots Lena becomes dimly aware somewhere in the recesses of her brain that the crowd is booing.

    It doesn’t help her situation, realistically. The crowd disagreeing with the decision doesn’t do much to allay the fact that Lena has well and truly been eliminated, but she gets some small satisfaction from the pure schadenfreude of Leslie’s sour expression when her apparent victory is overshadowed by the audience’s displeasure. She slouches backstage with Mike as the cameras focus in on the eliminated party, and Lena has no desire to speak as the show closes out.

    Kara manages to spout some tearful cliches over the ever-louder boos directed at the judges about being grateful for the experience and glad that it brought them together, but all Lena can see is a long, dark tunnel back to Metropolis. Alone.

    Neither of them speaks between exiting the stage and getting out of wardrobe. Nobody speaks to them, either – they’re left mostly alone, and Lena exits the dressing room to find Kara waiting for her with an expression that might be devastating if Lena was capable of feeling emotion right now. Kara says nothing as they walk together out of the building, hailing a car for Lena and wordlessly getting into it with her, and despite knowing she probably has missed calls from Sam Lena can’t bring herself to look at her phone. Nor can she bring herself to look at Kara. The numbness is comforting in its familiarity, but it’s equally frightening; the last time she went down this road, she didn’t like the person she became. It could be argued that Kara is the first person to have ever pulled her out of that dark place, and now she’s losing that, too.

    “So I suppose it’s time,” Lena says into the mantle of silence, pressing her fingernails hard into her palms of her hands. She can hear Kara’s head turn, but unlike their last cab ride she keeps her own eyes resolutely on the window. Rather than the shadowy shapes of the streets beyond all Lena can see is her own reflection, and her own coldness makes her stomach turn.

    “For what?” Kara’s voice is small. Devastated by their loss, probably. Lena’s resolve wavers; but if she doesn’t say this now, Kara is going to have to at some point. And hearing it from Kara might just shatter her.

    “To break up.”

    The silence is so heavy that Lena’s heartbeat sounds like a bass drum in her ears. She can hear Kara breathing, can hear her swallow and take an unsteady breath.

    “We should at least wait a few weeks, right?” Kara says, her voice sounding a little thick. “Otherwise it’s too obvious.”

    Lena knows that a few weeks is nothing in the grand scheme of things. At the end of those weeks, this is going to find its natural conclusion – they’ll go back to their normal lives in the end, and an extra month of pretending to date isn’t going to change that. But still, some synapse fires inside her at the prospect of not yet. A spark of emotion after her self-imposed shutdown.

    “Right. Yes, that’s a good idea,” Lena says, nodding along. “Make it look like we’re putting the effort in.”

    “I could even come see you in Metropolis. Take some pictures.”

    “Of course.”

    “It doesn’t have to end yet.”

    “No. Not yet.”

    Lena still hasn’t looked at Kara once.

    Kara doesn’t come up to her room like usual, just gives her a soft goodbye as she slides out of the car, and Lena rides the elevator up to her floor in silence. She makes a slow way to her soon-to-be former room in a haze with every intent to crawl into bed and not emerge until tomorrow afternoon when she’s sure her flight will be booked and ready.

    The door closes behind her, finally sealing her off from the outside world, and suddenly like a torturous Where’s Waldo book Lena notices a hundred tiny things that she’s been acclimated to all week. Kara’s things are scattered through the room like they’ve both been living there – a sweater thrown over the chair, socks near the end of the bed, scrunchies in every nook. Spare sweats, glasses, a phone charger, contact solution and a cheap dollar store toothbrush in the drawer of the bathroom vanity. A few of Kara’s DVDs are stacked next to the TV, and on top in a place of honour is the Wizard of Oz.

    Lena moves around it all, robotically undressing and washing the makeup from her face, but every reminder is another blow. The wall that’s been holding back the bulk of her struggle this week is cracking under the strain, and the final push that makes it crumble is when she sinks onto the bed to look at her phone.

    She does have a single missed call from Sam as well as two from Jess, likely an attempt to pre-plan her itinerary for leaving tomorrow, but that isn’t the issue – it’s seeing her lock screen. Kara had changed it to a photo of them together a few days ago claiming that it’s a common thing for couples to do, that it would help sell the ruse, and seeing her own smiling face next to Kara’s without a care in the world is what makes everything Lena has been desperately putting out of her mind escape in one terrible wave.

    It's like watching a floodgate open in the distance and being unable to move as she’s pulled into the dark undertow. The numbness gives way to too much sensation, too much emotion, and she has nothing to regulate it with. All the things she’d suppressed during the show - grief, anger, disappointment, shame - each gets their moment to pummel her as she drags herself between the sheets.

    This scenario hardly warrants a breakdown, and knowing it only makes her feel more pathetic; she and Kara aren’t actually dating, and this had to happen at some point. Despite her misgivings she’s let herself get too attached, and now absolutely losing it over having to return to a city she’s always loved living in and getting back to a life that, a few weeks ago, was perfectly comfortable. She wanted this when she first arrived. She wanted to be sent home. Now the reality of it feels too overwhelming.

    Her body curls in on itself, long-suppressed sobs clawing their way out of her without permission, and eventually Lena cries herself to exhaustion with Kara’s sweater balled up in the crook of her arm.

     

     

    Lena drifts in and out of a restless sleep for most of the night. Despite her body’s fatigue her mind refuses to shut off enough to get any decent rest, and when light starts to creep in through the curtains Lena finally gives up on the whole endeavour and dials Sam’s number.

    “Hey, you,” Sam answers on the second ring, the rustling and clattering noises in the background making Lena suspect she’s probably getting ready for work. “I was wondering when you’d get back to me.” There’s sympathy in her voice before Lena has even spoken, and all the shame she tried to sleep through roosts in Lena’s chest again.

    “You didn’t leave any messages,” Lena notes. Sam hums noncommittally.

    “I figured you needed time to lick your wounds before you would want to talk.”

    “You were right. As usual.” Lena sighs heavily, willing the weight in her chest to ease as she rolls over and puts an arm over her eyes to block the light out. She’s dehydrated and sore, and she’s too morose to drag herself out of bed to get some water.

    “Sorry for having such a big brain,” Sam says, but the attempt humour doesn’t quite land. Lena sniffles and rubs at her still-congested nose.

    “How’s the media outlook? Did my terrible routine tank our stocks yet?” Lena asks, trying not to sound as despondent as she feels. Sam snorts.

    “Hah! The opposite, actually. People are calling this elimination the worst decision in the show’s history.”

    Lena pauses. Her self-pity momentarily on hold, she even laughs a little. “That’s a bit dramatic, isn’t it?”

    “No, I think it’s the exact right amount of drama,” Sam says, and Lena can hear the jingle of keys as she assumedly leaves for the day. “People really loved you guys.”

    “I’ll have to take a look myself.” Lena takes a few long breaths through her mouth, in and out slowly until she feels a little calmer. “I should never have done this.”

    “We achieved what we set out to,” Sam insists, raising her voice over the sudden sounds of the Metropolis streets. A car honks loudly, and Sam’s voice gets distant for a second as she yells at the driver. “Hey, fucko! The light is red, so back off!”

    “Your image has never been better,” Sam continues calmly a second later, having miraculously crossed the street safely. “You couldn’t have paid for this kind of public facelift.”

    Lena doesn’t answer. She just sniffs again, casting around for a Kleenex and coming up empty, and Sam gentles her voice.

    “The time you spent with Kara is a good memory. Doesn’t it outweigh the sucky part, even a little?”

    “Not now,” Lena admits, fully aware of exactly how ridiculous she sounds after having insisted to Sam not long ago that her feelings for Kara were completely platonic. “Not when I have to leave her.”

    There’s no sense in trying to lie to Sam anymore. The show is over; Sam would probably have dragged the confession out of her over a few bottles of wine as soon as she landed on the east coast anyways, and Lena is far from having any pride left at this point.

    “Oh, honey,” Sam murmurs, and her sympathy makes Lena’s eyes water again. “You’re down worse than I thought. I’m sorry that I pushed you to do this.”

    “I could have put my foot down. I didn’t,” Lena says plainly. “Instead I got attached to someone completely unavailable to me.” It’s an objective fact, and apparently her breakdown last night stripped her of all pretense.

    “It’s not like you’ll never see her again.”

    “It’s exactly like that, actually,” Lena argues, vocalizing for the first time what she’s been mulling over in her mind all week. “She lives across the country. Maybe at first she’ll want to visit, but eventually she’ll get bored and her interest will shift. I’m not enough of a draw to keep something like that up. It’s not a sustainable relationship.”

    “Aren’t you sort of underestimating how much she-”

    Lena misses the end of Sam’s sentence when her call waiting beeps insistently in her ear, and she checks the caller ID with a frown. Kara’s contact photo is staring back at her, the picture she took what feels like eons ago of Kara pretending to eat the cartoon bowl of rice on the menu at her favourite Chinese restaurant.

    “Sorry. Call waiting,” Lena says before Sam can repeat herself, and Sam makes a disbelieving noise.

    “Who’s calling you at 5am besides me?”

    “Kara,” Lena says, her thumb already hovering distractedly over the green button. She’s talked to Kara on the phone countless times now, but after the strangeness of last night the idea of it is making her heart pound. Now is as good an opportunity as any to clear the air with Kara before she flies home.

    “Ah,” Sam says knowingly, and the street noises in the background turn into the familiar but strangely distant hubbub of the L-Corp lobby. “You should take it, then. I’ll meet you at the airport later, okay?”

    Sam hangs up, and Lena answers Kara’s call with an unsteady hand.

    “Hey. Sorry, I was on the phone with Sam,” Lena starts, screwing her eyes shut and mustering up her courage. “About last night…Kara, I -”

    “We’re back!”

    Kara’s voice is blares loud and excited through the speaker, totally incongruous with Lena’s mood, and Lena’s words die on her lips.

    “…what?” she asks instead, rolling over and propping herself up on an elbow. She tucks the phone more securely against her ear, and Kara’s voice only gets louder.

    “I have no idea how it happened, but they just asked me if we would be willing to go back on the show,” Kara says, sounding breathless. “To be part of the top 5.”

    “You’re saying we aren’t eliminated?” Lena asks, her mind reeling. She’d been all set to apologize for freezing Kara out, thank her for all her help, and ask to go their separate ways. But now Kara is telling her they’re still in the running? It seems too good to be true.

    “No, we were eliminated,” Kara corrects, and Lena can almost see her pacing the floor of her apartment. “Now they just want us back. Are you in?”

    “Why would they want us back? And how did this all happen before 6 in the morning?”

    “I didn’t ask,” Kara says simply, her energy buzzing even through the phone. “I just called you. I was too excited. I didn’t really sleep last night, so I was already up when they called.”

    As much as Lena’s heart tries to leap right to Kara at the offer, Lena doesn’t answer right away.

    She needs to be rational about this. Last night was as clear an indication as can be that this has already gone too far – her infatuation with Kara is too advanced, and she needs to extricate herself. It’s not even in danger anymore of becoming something she’s truly terrified of; it’s already there. An all-consuming kind of devotion that she hasn’t let herself tip into in a long time. And beyond that, every day that they keep up the façade of a romantic relationship is another day where they could be exposed for faking it. Another day Andrea could find a weakness in their united front and blow the whole thing up. The logical thing to do would be to say no, and take the escape hatch she’s been given before this whole thing implodes and can’t be salvaged. Thank Kara for everything she’s done and gracefully exit with some of her dignity intact.

    “What do you say?” Kara asks, a little calmer but still so enthusiastic that Lena is surprised she hasn’t sprinted to the hotel to convince her in person. “Want to make them all sorry they sent us home?”

    Lena looks at her half-packed bags, at Kara’s things littered around her room and the crumpled NCU sweater still tangled with her in the blankets, and thinks about the empty apartment and stack of work she’s heading back to. A life without Kara.

    There are a litany of answers in her head, most of them reasonable, but the least logical of them is the only one that feels right.

    “I’m in.”

    Lena has to move the phone away from her ear to avoid hearing damage when Kara’s shouts her approval at top volume.

    After ending the call with a promise to meet for rehearsal early to get a jump start, Lena flops back onto the bed and stares at the swirls in the popcorn ceiling. Everything looks the same as it did a few minutes ago, the same clean hotel furniture and dim light of dawn, but it’s all changed irrevocably.

    They have more time. And if last night told Lena anything, it’s that she’s getting more attached to Kara by the day. There might come a time when ending this will truly break her, and it gets closer with each week she stays here.

    “Fuck,” Lena whispers, torn between delighted laughter and total panic. “What am I doing?”

    Getting out of bed and starting on the morning routine that seemed like an unclimbable mountain before Kara’s call feels so simple, now. She practically jumps up, digging things out of her suitcases and turning on the TV to fill the quiet, and the third channel she clicks to is the same morning show she’d been a guest on not long ago. The one that started this whole business. The two hosts are bantering back and forth, and Lena almost flicks right past their overly cheerful faces until she sees the Dancing with the Stars logo pop up on the screen behind them.

    “Now, everyone was devastated when our favourite Dancing with the Stars couple got eliminated last week,” the female host is saying, and Lena turns the volume up a few notches. “Lena and Kara were sent home during partner swap week, and I think I speak for everyone when I say it was a shock!”

    The footage behind them shows Lena’s fall in slow-motion from the perspective of the cameras, and Lena winces at the reminder. But this time she sees something she didn’t before – the camera switches to Kara at the side of the stage at the moment of impact.     Disqualified. That certainly explains why the show would have asked for her and Kara to return – the slow needs to be filled, and as the last to go home they make the most sense. Lena can’t imagine what Leslie could have done to get herself kicked off the show, but she’s distracted from speculating by the screen filling with tweets.

    ‘last eliminated should be first back, it’s only fair. no I am not personally invested in this as a lesbian why do you ask’

    ‘if #dwts doesn’t bring back Lena and Kara, we burn the studio down’

    ‘gays gather round, we need a prayer circle for our girls to come back!!’

    The last one makes Lena choke on air.

    ‘if I don’t get to see kara spiritually blowing lena luthor’s back out on stage again, I might actually die ☹’

The most she’s been privy to are the major articles and the sheer amount of engagement she gets every time she remembers to make an Instagram post.

    Lena jumps in the shower before the show cuts to commercial.

     

     

    She ends up being over an hour early, too antsy to stay in her hotel room for much longer even after a lengthy logistics call with Jess, and Kara isn’t there yet when Lena arrives at the studio. Usually she doesn’t mind spending a little extra time outside waiting, but since there’s a persistent drizzle hanging over the city this morning she heads into the building and up the stairs alone to wait in the hall instead.

A peek inside reveals Mike as the culprit – he’s rapping along with the lyrics (pretty badly in Lena’s opinion, but she’s not a connoisseur of the genre) as he cleans out his things for the season. He seems oddly cheerful for someone who was just disqualified from the competition.

    “Lena! Hey!” he shouts, but Lena can barely hear him over the music. When she points at her ears he seems to realize, and he pulls out his phone to hit pause. “Sorry! Didn’t realize anyone else was here.”

    “It’s fine, I’m just monstrously early,” Lena drawls, leaning against the doorframe. Mike throws the speaker into a box, folding up the table and carrying it over to a storage closet.

    “Excited to get back to it?” he grunts, heaving the table inside and closing the door. He dusts off his hands, grinning boyishly. “I bet Kara’s chomping at the bit.”

    “So you heard?”

    Mike blows a raspberry. “Everyone did. Leslie was fired right after the show, and she came to the bar looking to fight about it. Alex managed to calm her down and get her to leave.”

    Lena can only imagine how that might have gone. If it weren’t for Alex’s job being tied to the show, Lena would bet good money on Alex dominating in a fight between the two; as it is she’s glad the situation was de-escalated.

And now that he’s mentioned them, she’s reminded of something she found strange but had been too stressed to think about at the time; a distinct memory of Leslie backstage just before their Disney performance, slipping something into her pocket. The photos had appeared right after.

    Lena stops. Mike keeps going for a few steps, turning around when he realizes she’s fallen behind, and Lena stares after him in disbelief.

    “Leslie told me everything before she went back onstage with Kara yesterday, and I met with the bigwigs after the show,” Mike shrugs, shifting his grip on the box. “She seemed to need to gloat to someone. And she didn’t cover her tracks very well.”

    “Kara is a good person,” Mike says, and his obvious affection for Kara doesn’t strike up any jealousy in her. Lena had thought when she first saw him dance that he’d be smarmy and irritating, especially after finding out he was Kara’s ex, but it turns out that despite his more annoying traits it’s strangely difficult to dislike him. “She’s always thinking of others before herself. Did she ever tell you that we dated?”

    “She did.”

    “We weren’t well matched. But instead of getting mad when I was a shit boyfriend, she set me up with Imra as soon as we started having issues,” Mike explains, his smile getting soft and fond. “She could have rightfully been mad at Lucy when Luce fell for James while they were together, but she stepped out of the way. She even hosted them an engagement party last year.”

    It’s an incredibly Kara thing to do, but now that Lena knows her a little better that sentiment has a deeper meaning to it. Kara might have hidden it well but she was probably hurt by the breakup, and still she put everyone else first. Even to her own detriment.

    “She always steps out of the way for everyone else,” Mike continues, strangely observantly for a man that Lena always tagged as mostly a pretty face. “She deserves to get what she wants for once. I guess I’m trying to pay her back, for all of us.”

    “How so?”

    He looks thoughtful for a second, his thick brows furrowing, but his expression clears when he finds his answer.

    “I’m stepping out of the way.”

    He heads down the stairs without any further explanation. His quick footsteps echo, and Lena jogs to the top of them and shouts down after him.

    “But…what does that mean?”

    He rounds the corner and waves goodbye without answering.

    Lena never makes it to the lunchroom. She stands there for a minute or so, staring down the stairs with Mike’s cryptic words whirling around in her head, and she’s only startled out of her reverie when Kara appears at the bottom. Kara’s face lights up the moment she sees Lena, and she bounds up the stairs to scoop her into a big, spinning hug on the landing. Kara holds her for longer than usual, squeezes tighter, and when she sets Lena down again it’s as invigorating as her morning tea.

    “Second chance week,” Kara says, pressing a kiss to Lena’s forehead and then rushing to unlock the door to their studio. Their room. “Let’s not waste it, right?”

    Lena couldn’t agree more. Her forehead tingles in the place Kara’s lips just were; even though she knows she’s hurtling towards an even worse heartbreak than she felt last night once the show ends for real, even that knowledge can’t override how good it feels to be here with Kara in the moment. Her future self will probably look back at this and hate her for her selfishness - her present self, once again on the receiving end of Kara’s sunlight, really doesn’t care.

    HERE WE GO
