Touching Kara has become such an unconscious thing now that she doesn’t even remember doing it, even a touch so blatantly intimate as that, but the evidence is staring her in the face. And on-screen, her interview-self is staring at Kara’s face with such obvious affection that it borders on devotion.

    “It would be great to win, but even if we don’t make it, I feel like I lucked out,” Screen-Kara says, turning to look at Lena with a soft smile. “I met Lena. That’s pretty amazing in itself.”

    Lena remembers acutely how butterflies had erupted in her chest when Kara said that on the day. The surety behind it, the absolute gratefulness in her voice. Some of the butterflies seem to have survived all the way to this moment - she can still feel them beating their wings against her heart.

    As it transitions into rehearsal footage Kara shifts a bit, whispering directly in Lena’s ear in the darkness.

    “You know I meant that, right?”

    “Meant what?” Lena whispers back.

    “Whatever happens tonight, whether we get in the top 3 or not, dancing with you has been one of the best experiences of my life,” Kara says, low but earnest. Her voice cracks a little. “I will always be so, so glad that I met you.”

    It’s too earnest for Lena to set aside. In the limbo between privacy and performance Kara has decided to string together the perfect sequence of words to blast through a wall Lena has been trying desperately to fortify all week, and they’re seconds away from the video ending.

    “Me, too,” Lena manages to say, the words tumbling out of the rubble Kara’s left behind. “Thank you. For everything.”

    “You don’t need to thank me. I’d do anything for you, Lena.”

    It feels very much like there’s something else Kara is trying to say, something behind the words she’s allowed herself. Lena can guess at what it is purely based on the soft, lingering kiss Kara presses to her temple with exactly the same tenderness she did when Lena fell asleep in her bed, but there’s no time to interrogate it - another wrecking ball hits her ribcage, and Lena is still being thrown about in that particular emotional hurricane when the lights come up like a sunrise.

    Usually Lena would characterize the trance-like state she goes into on stage as something like an autopilot. Muscle memory takes over and she generally flies through the routines with Kara by relying on the sheer amount of practice they’ve done throughout the week. When they end she’s never fully sure how she got through it, besides focusing on Kara alone and letting herself be guided. This time, from the first note of the song, Lena is completely in manual. Autopilot doesn’t take the wheel – she’s in full control of her body, hitting every move intentionally with a grace she didn’t know she possessed, and she channels every ounce of whatever Kara’s words have triggered into it.

    She can feel Kara right there with her in a way she’s been resisting all week. They’re in perfect sync as they jump and spin side by side, crashing into each other in controlled yet dramatic, unpredictable ways; every flourish, every heartbeat is aligned. Lena doesn’t hesitate in the moments of intimacy anymore. She lets her body become pure emotion. Everything Kara has been saying in rehearsal this week – since the very beginning, really – about fluidity and connection and improvisation makes sense. The routine is no longer just a series of movements she’s memorized. It’s a conversation between them.

    She’s mid-lift and whirling through the air with Kara’s hands strong on her back and upper arm when she finally understands what everyone has been trying to tell her.

    After putting all her trust in Andrea, the one person her younger self never thought could turn on her, she’s always considered that part of herself irrevocably broken. Even Sam and Jess, her only close friends, she keeps a great deal of things from. Things she’s shared with Kara with hardly a thought. She trusts Kara. She trusts Kara completely – she trusts her with her secrets, her safety, her body. Her heart. She trusts Kara to be different.

    She lands lightly on her feet and in a series of choreographed movements they chase each other to the setpiece on the right side of the stage. The scaffold they’ve been rehearsing on is now styled to look like a cliff face to match the field-of-wildflowers romance novel vibe of the dance, mostly paper-mache and painted Styrofoam but adequately convincing at a distance to conceal the platform and stairs underneath. Lena remembers the moves they’ve been working on since they decided to get rid of the lift. She remembers them, but in the forefront of her mind now are the original steps. The ones that have her jumping off the scaffold and into Kara. Here, in the exhilarating heat of the moment, it doesn’t frighten her at all.

    Improvise, urges a voice inside her that sounds like Kara. I promise, I am going to be here to catch you.

    Kara notices the deviation as soon as Lena takes the first step onto the stairs. There’s confusion in her face, but between steps the decision passes between them wordlessly – acceptance. Excitement. Encouragement. Kara adapts and moves into position, flawlessly switching with Lena to the old choreography that they haven’t practiced since Wednesday, and at the top of the cliff Lena lets go like she couldn’t then.

    The memory of falling on this stage with Mike is a distant dream. This isn’t Mike. It isn’t Andrea or Lex or anyone else who’s ever pulled the floor out from under her. This is Kara. There’s a total understanding between them, a trust on a molecular level. Despite never having managed the lift in its entirety she jumps, soaring through the space between them and executing a perfect twist mid-air to land in Kara’s arms for a wide twirl, secure and unharmed, and Kara’s pride radiates from her like a halo as they transfer into a mutual roll for the floor-work that finishes the routine. Kara is trembling when she pulls Lena’s back against herself for the final moments, arms wrapped tightly around her torso from behind and her hands gripping Lena’s dress like they’re desperate for any point of contact.

    “Lena, I -” Kara’s breath hitches in Lena’s ear, a hesitation and then a surrender Lena can hear even over the music and the pounding of her heart. “Lena.”

    The final notes play. Kara pulls her impossibly closer.

    “I love you.”

    Lena feels those three words in every nerve ending. Forgetting the choreography and the stage entirely she twists just enough to see Kara’s face looking down at her. There’s no trace of acting in her eyes. They’re shiny under the lights, wide and blue and shimmering, and their lips are so close – Kara’s breath is warm and inviting and everything narrows to what Lena wants more than anything. What she’s wanted from the start.

    The ground beneath her shifts when their lips meet.

    Lena’s barriers were lying in ruin before they even started dancing and now Kara simply steps over the foundations and right into Lena’s heart, kissing her soft at first but turning firm and insistent when Lena wraps a hand around the back of Kara’s neck. It’s everything it should be, everything Lena expected and feared – butterflies, fireworks, electricity, chemistry. All the descriptors that have haunted her since they met swirling together at once and keeping them together like magnets. But the terror Lena had built up around this moment never materializes as Kara’s mouth slants against hers, the barest hint of tongue turning into something deep and open-mouthed. It feels more inevitable than anything else. Like settling into something tailor-made and perfectly comfortable after a lifetime of constriction. Something like home.

    All of that disappears when they part for air, their eyes locked, and the reality of the situation hits her in an unrelenting wall of noise.

    The screaming is ear-splitting. The crowd is louder now than it’s ever been, the lights brighter, the cameras more intrusive. Brainy is hovering nearby seeming unsure whether or not he should break them up, and she can see more than one fan in the first few rows near the edge of the stage hysterically crying. And Lena is frozen, still half-convinced she hallucinated the whole thing.

    But she didn’t hallucinate it. She just kissed Kara. She kissed Kara on live television, and Kara’s confession is somehow even louder in her memory than the noise of the audience.

    It’s Kara who takes charge. She lets go of Lena and hops to her feet, helping Lena up with an easy pull of her hand, and laughs off Brainy’s vamping about the passion of the moment while Lena’s lipstick is still smudged all over her mouth. From the kiss. The kiss she can still feel tingling on her own lips, which they shared in front of the entire world.

    Lena doesn’t hear a word of the comments from the judges. She’s completely unaware of their score, and the audience fades into white noise. All she can think about is what Kara said, and the voice she said it in. How Kara’s lips felt. When the comments are over, all she hears is I love you; when Kara leads her towards the wings, Lena’s legs barely obey her commands. She’s just clinging to the back of Kara’s damp shirt as if the pressure of her grip on the fabric can communicate to Kara what her words can’t seem to, and she’s only vaguely aware of the pointed, significant look Andrea gives her when they pass each other in the wings as she and James head out for their number.

    She and Kara are left backstage while Andrea’s video starts in the distance, two bastions in a sea of mid-broadcast chaos.

    “Lena,” Kara breathes. Lena’s heart is hammering. There’s more meaning in those two syllables than there’s ever been before, and Kara wants to talk about it. Here. Now.

    “That was…” Kara starts, before her voice dies. “I don’t -”

    Kara hesitates again, licking her dry lips. Lena can’t stop looking at them. Seeing the smudged evidence of her own lips spread across them and remembering how they tasted. Half of her wants to bolt for the nearest exit; half of her is rooted to the floor, waiting on Kara’s next words.

    “I don’t think it was ever fake, for me.”

    Some small, unconscious sound wrests itself from Lena’s lungs, but no actual reply. She knows what she wants to say. She knows what she needs to say. It wasn’t fake for me, either. Please, please can we talk somewhere private. The admission she’s been holding back for weeks swells in her ribcage, rises in her throat, but it won’t come out.

    She’s thrumming with adrenaline and terror, throttled by the last vestiges of her own fears. Choking on words she hasn’t said in so long that it feels like they’re trapped under a layer of rust, and as much as she trusts Kara it feels impossible to pry them out here in the middle of the studio with these people all around. Techs and assistants and sound engineers are rushing past them, and she can see at least two cameras in their vicinity. Neither are pointed at them but the threat is there, the promise of their conversation being captured at any moment - her heart is beating so loudly that she’s sure their mics can pick it up even at a distance. She’s not sure Kara even realizes where they are or what’s going on around them, but Lena can’t breathe.

    There are too many people. Too much happening for her to make any kind of conscious decision right now. And she wishes more than anything that Kara had just waited until they were alone.

    Kara’s voice breaks when she speaks again.

    “Lena?”

    Kara is asking – begging – for something to hold onto. Something Lena wants desperately to give before it’s too late, but her mouth is dry and empty.

    “Hey guys, could you move further backstage?” A harried tech interrupts, blazing right through whatever Lena might have managed to say. “Miss Rojas is going to need wardrobe when the routine ends.”

    Lena moves on wobbly legs, trying to leave more space near the wings, but Kara doesn’t follow. The look of pure devastation on her face stops Lena in her tracks.

    “I shouldn’t have said anything.”

    “Kara,” Lena whispers, but her voice dies when someone else walks by with a large boom mic. She can’t quite seem to make it start again.

    “I’m an idiot. I just completely fucked everything up, didn’t I?” Kara says, running both hands through her hair with a high, stuttering sort of laugh. “I can’t believe I - ignore me. Fuck. Just pretend I never said anything.”

    She’s hardly ever heard Kara swear before, and it’s shocking enough that Lena can finally speak as Kara is walking – more like sprinting, really – away from her and towards the dressing rooms.

    “Kara, wait!” Lena calls, her voice cracking. “Please!”

    But Kara is gone. She’s gone, having made herself intensely vulnerable in a way Lena wasn’t brave enough to do, and Lena just let her hit the ground.

    “Fuck,” Lena whispers, pressing a hand hard against her chest until it starts to hurt and it feels like she can breathe again. “God damn it.”

    Lena does follow after a moment, for all the good it does. She looks for Kara in the precious minutes they have before they’re due back onstage, but Kara knows the building far better than Lena ever will. By the time Lena has looked in the limited handful of places she can access she’s already missed the score announcements, and she doesn’t see Kara again until Alex is shoving them back onstage for the final 3 decision. Kara’s makeup looks freshly applied, her eyes ever so slightly red-rimmed.

    Lena grabs for her hand as they walk to their place, but it’s stiff in her grasp. No comforting squeeze. Kara still puts an arm around her while they wait onstage like she always does, is still smiling for the cameras, but it doesn’t meet her eyes. And her eyes never meet Lena’s. She stares ahead towards the crowd or the judges or the cameras – anywhere but to her left.

    Lena doesn’t even hear who goes home and who makes the top 3. She and Kara could have been eliminated for all she knows. It’s all window-dressing, a blur in the background. All she knows is that Kara disappears again – despite Lena’s weak protests – as soon as the lights power down, and she’s left in limbo near the dressing rooms.

    “You know, I think that somehow went even worse than I thought it would.”

    Alex is at her elbow, her headset nowhere to be found and her face set in a grimace. Lena doesn’t have the energy to return the serve when Alex nudges her past the dressing rooms and into a small private office.

    “I know,” Lena says heavily, sinking into a chair that Alex pulls out for her. There’s a framed photo of Alex and Kelly on the desk, and a strip of photo booth pictures with Kara on a bulletin board next to a list of phone extensions. Kara is beaming in them, but her static smile doesn’t override the heartbroken expression that’s now seared into Lena’s memory. “I broke our agreement. I fucked it all up. I’m sorry.”

    “That’s not exactly what I was -”

    “I don’t know how to fix it,” Lena pushes on, desperation cracking her voice. She feels lost. Adrift and unsure. “She just – she said it in the middle of the broadcast, and I didn’t have time to explain. Did she go home?”

    “Explain what?”

    Lena’s mouth goes dry.

    “If you can’t tell me, you’re not going to be able to tell her,” Alex says more gently. “Consider me a practice run. Explain what, Lena?”

    The softer tone helps, as does the privacy. Lena swallows. “Explain that I…”

    After another false start, Alex huffs. “Come on. You two have been in love with each other since the first week and you can’t say it even once?”

    “That’s rich, coming from you!” Lena snaps back, her temper finally sparking back to life at the audacity of Alex, of all people, pressing her to say what she really feels. “You’re the one who told me to be careful!”

    “I meant that you two should have your emotional shit sorted out before you impulsively have sex, not that you shouldn’t get together at all!”

    “Oh,” Lena says, the wind thoroughly taken out of her sails. The following quiet is intensely awkward.

    “I should have let Kelly do this,” Alex groans, burying her face in her hands. She digs her nails briefly into her forehead before sitting up straight again to look Lena in the eye, a line of crescent-shaped marks dotting her brow. “I like you, Lena. You make Kara happy, and you’re not totally insufferable to be around.”

    “Wow. Am I blushing?” Lena mutters.

    “But,” Alex interrupts loudly, “it’s pretty obvious from what just happened that if you two had hooked up last week, you would have freaked the fuck out in the morning and broken her heart. Am I wrong?”

    Lena would like to deny it, but she can’t. It’s true. Had she woken up in bed with Kara after the party last week, there’s a good chance she would have been on a plane back to Metropolis by noon. She hadn’t been ready. She’s fought this for so long because she hasn’t been ready this whole time. But maybe she is now.

    “Well maybe you should have said that instead!” Lena huffs.

    “Well maybe –“ Alex stops mid-sentence, breathing deep in a way Lena is sure was taught to her by Kelly. “Okay. Okay, I was cryptic, that’s on me.”

    She hadn’t been expecting Alex to admit it so readily, and Lena’s frustration deflates a little.

    “Let me be clear this time,” Alex says, steepling her fingers together and staring at Lena over the top. “She is crazy about you. Head over freaking heels. But if you’re going to go to her right now you should be sure of what you want when you get there.”

    Lena wants to do just that. She wants to seek Kara out and tell her everything, unload the whole mess of her thoughts on her doorstep just to get it out of her own system. But Alex is right again. Kara deserves decisiveness. “It’s not that easy. I can’t just erase years worth of – of emotional shit in an instant just because I have feelings for her.”

    “You just admitted you have feelings for her, so that’s step one,” Alex sighs. “What is it that you’re so afraid of?”

    “Having feelings for her doesn’t guarantee a good outcome. If I end up hurting her…” Lena says, her breath catching at the reminder that she’s already done so. Kara is hurting as they speak, and it’s her fault. It feels like a knife to the ribs.

    “Have you ever heard of a self-fulfilling prophecy?” Alex says frankly. “That ship has sailed. You can’t guarantee a good outcome in any relationship, Lena. It’s not a math equation. All you can do is try.”

    All she can do is try. She can try, can’t she? For Kara. Trying can’t be any worse than what she’s already done, running so blindly that she sprinted headfirst into the thing she was trying to avoid. Twisting herself up so much over it that she basically ensured it would happen.

    “I really should have talked to you earlier,” Lena says. Alex rolls her eyes.

    “I doubt you would have listened to me any more than Kara did,” Alex says, but she’s smiling over her fingers. “So. Do you want to be with her?”

    “Yes,” Lena says, falling back against her seat when it finally, finally comes out without hesitation. It’s like the easing of a migraine, like the reprieve of the knot Kara rubbed out of her back. A lifetime of pain dropping out into a wave of incredible relief. She could almost laugh. “Yes, I do. I want to be with her.”

    “So go,” Alex says loudly, and the urgency propels Lena up and out of her chair. “She’s at her apartment. Do Sam and I have to do everything around here?”

    Lena skids to a halt at the open door.

    “You’ve been talking to Sam?”

    “Go!”

     

     

    Lena has never been more thankful for the broken keycard system on Kara’s apartment building. It means she doesn’t have to wait for some other tenant to take pity and let her in – she can just hit the handle at the same angle she’s seen Kara do countless times and wait for the beep to slip in herself, taking the stairs two at a time until she’s standing on Kara’s colourful welcome mat and facing the familiar 4-A label on her door.

    It's only when her knuckles are hovering over the wood that she realizes she’s still wearing her stage costume. She hadn’t thought to go home and change; getting here was too important.

    She knocks three times, and waits.

    “Alex, I told you I don’t want to talk about it.”

    Kara’s voice is heavy, even muffled. It tugs at Lena’s heart. She swallows past the knot it makes in her chest.

    “It’s me.”

    Silence. Lena can’t even hear Kara moving beyond the door. She’d almost think Kara might have climbed out the window and escaped to the roof if she didn’t press her ear to it, where Kara’s reply is almost too quiet to pick up.

    “I really need to be alone right now. I’m sorry.”

    “I know,” Lena says, matching Kara’s volume, shifting to tilt her forehead against the wood. “Can I just talk to you for a second?”

    Kara takes her time to reply again. When she speaks her voice is closer, like she’s standing right on the other side of the door and echoing Lena’s position. Lena puts a hand up against the surface, pressing her palm to it. Wondering if Kara is doing the same.

    “I’ll be normal again by tomorrow. I promise,” Kara says, something ragged tangled up in her voice. “But right now I just…I can’t pretend. I need some time.” She sounds defeated. Broken. I’ll be normal again by tomorrow – a heart-wrenching reminder that Kara has been suppressing this for most of the time they’ve known each other, wearing a mask. They both have. A reminder that Lena has hurt her terribly, and yet she’s still being so gentle.

    Kara has every right to ignore Lena completely right now to nurse her wounds alone, but she’s still putting Lena first.

    “Kara, please,” Lena says softly, trying to pour into her tone everything she couldn’t say earlier. “I don’t want to do this out in the hallway, but I will if I have to. If you don’t like what I have to say I promise I’ll leave.”

    She hears Kara sigh. The lock clicks, and Kara opens the door enough for Lena to slip through.

    Unlike Lena, Kara has changed out of her costume. She’s in threadbare sweats and the NCU sweater she recently liberated from Lena’s hotel room, the sleeves pulled down to cover her hands. Her hair is piled in a messy bun, her eyes red and still wet with recent tears behind her smudged glasses - tissues are scattered across the coffee table next to a mug of hot chocolate and a disassembled photo frame, the photo within lying on a couch cushion like Kara had been holding it when she arrived. Lena can guess which one it is.

    Kara herself won’t meet Lena’s eyes. She’s looking diligently somewhere to the right, chewing on her lip with her arms tightly folded, and Lena gathers her thoughts as best she can.

    “Firstly…I’m sorry. About tonight,” Lena starts, twisting her hands together. “I’ve never been good at saying what’s on my mind, and I was so overwhelmed from the stage and everyone being around us -”

    “You have nothing to apologize for. I was out of line,” Kara says, closing her eyes. Her brow is furrowed, and Lena wants to smooth it so badly that she digs her nails into the back of her opposite hand.

    “No, you weren’t. I should have said something.”

    “What could you have said?” Kara asks tiredly. “I get it, Lena. It’s fine. Let’s just forget it.”

    “I don’t want to forget it.”

    “If you want to stop pretending to date, I completely understand,” Kara says tonelessly, her eyes casting down towards the floor. It’s clear that Kara isn’t actually listening, too intent on hurrying Lena out the door so she can be alone again, and it might take more than a stumbling explanation for Lena to get her point across.

    Kara isn’t looking at Lena at all. Her arms are crossed, her posture defeated. Lena feels a spark of frustration up her spine. Of all the times for Kara to stop listening to her, she’s chosen the most inconvenient.

    “Well, yes. I do want to stop pretending to date,” Lena says.

    Kara sighs heavily, her apparent suspicion confirmed. Her mouth twists into a broken line. “Right. Great. So, uh. That’s that, I guess. If that’s all, we have an early morning tomorrow -” Kara says, blinking rapidly.

    “I don’t care,” Lena says. “Please, can you just hear me out?”

    “ – and I really need some time to be alone – “ Kara is continuing, eyes still fixed on the floor. Lena feels the infuriating urge to stomp her feet.

    “Kara, stop. Look at me,” Lena says sharply, injecting a hint of her boardroom voice into a command that Kara responds to immediately. Kara’s spine straightens and she makes the briefest amount of eye contact, enough for Lena to see the tears hovering on her eyelashes. Seeing the actual evidence of her heartbreak, Lena takes a fortifying breath. She’d really prefer they were doing this sitting down, but Kara isn’t budging and it’s only fair that this happen on her comfort level.

    “You…you terrify me,” Lena starts, digging her thumb hard into her opposite hand to ground herself. “I’ve spent years making sure I wouldn’t ever be hurt again, and letting that go is very difficult. So I - “

    Of course, Kara interrupts. “I’m sorry I betrayed your trust, kissing you on stage like that. And I’ll try to make up for it later. But I’m not really in a place right now to – “

    This time Lena really does stomp her foot.

    “Damn it, Kara, will you shut up and listen to me? I’m not upset with you!”

    “You sound pretty upset,” Kara mumbles, her hands flexing on her crossed arms. Lena grits her teeth. She’d imagined this to be considerably more romantic than it’s turning out to be, and she’s one more disruption away from throwing explaining out the door and just kissing the interruptions from Kara’s lips.

    “Because you’re not hearing what I’m saying!”

    “It’s really late, can you please not yell so loud? My neighbour is this sweet old lady, and I don’t want her to – ”

    Lena doesn’t think before she grabs Kara forcibly by the fabric of her sweatshirt. Kara startles, eyes wide, looking up and down from Lena’s face to her hands and back - but Lena beats her to the punch before she can interrupt yet again.

    “Kara,” Lena says very clearly, enunciating every syllable, “I want to date you.”

    Kara opens her mouth, obviously still about to come out with whatever she was going to say before Lena pre-empted her - but miraculously, she freezes. Her mouth widens, and then snaps shut.

    “What?”

    “I said,” Lena says more gently, taking advantage of Kara’s newly-full attention and letting go of her sweater to instead take one of her hands, “that I want to date you, Kara. And I’m so sorry I didn’t say that before now.”

    “You want to date me,” Kara says.

    “Yes.”

    “Like, for real?” Kara’s disbelief is almost cartoonish, her voice clearing and her eyes suddenly brighter, and Lena laughs.

    “Yes.” 

    “Right. Right. Okay.” Kara finally seems to realize that Lena is holding her hand, and she squeezes it hard as if to convince herself it’s really there. “Uh…are you sure?”

    Lena laughs harder. She tugs a little until Kara is standing closer, relief and giddiness making her feel light as a feather after the frustration of the night. Kara’s hair is slowly coming out of its bun, wavy blonde wisps falling around her face, and Lena tucks one behind her ear.  “Is that somehow hard to believe?”

    “Kinda, yeah! A few hours ago I thought you were rejecting me.”

    “In fairness I did not actually reject you,” Lena points out, tapping a finger against Kara’s chest with her free hand. “I froze, and you ran.”

    “…yeah, I guess I did,” Kara admits after a pause. She grabs the hand in question and holds it against her chest. “You just looked so scared, and I thought…”

    “I know,” Lena says, calmed by the low vibration of Kara’s chest under her hand while she talks. “We’re both going to have to work on that if we want to make this work.”

    “This?”

    “This.” Lena bites her lip, letting it slide between her teeth before she puts it all out on the table. “I’ve been fighting off feelings for you since the second we met. Dancing with you is the most alive I’ve ever felt. I just needed to be brave enough to admit it.” 

    “Golly,” Kara whispers. She winces over the word choice as soon as it leaves her mouth, but Lena is so stupidly endeared that she might have kissed Kara right then if she didn’t continue talking. “Um. I guess I should have let you finish, huh?”

    “We got there in the end.”

    “When I told you that earlier,” Kara says, her voice cracking, “when I said…you know. The look on your face, I thought...”

    “You thought you’d become a burden.”

    Kara nods. Her grip on Lena’s hand tightens, and Lena squeezes right back.

    “I didn’t freeze because of your big feelings,” Lena says. It feels important to make this distinction – Kara should never feel like a burden. Not ever. “It was because of mine. Everything that happened with Andrea was so long ago, but it’s made any thought of a real relationship feel impossible. You’ve taught me to trust myself. My own ability. And I trust you.” Lena takes a big breath, letting it out evenly. “Which is big, for me. As is admitting I might have gone about things the wrong way.”

    Kara laughs. It sounds almost like a hiccup, a little tearful but mostly happy.  “I liked you from the moment I saw your audition tape. I suggested this whole fake thing because I…I just wanted to be closer to you. Spend time with you. And the more time I spent with you, the harder it got to pretend I didn’t want all of it to be real.”

    “I was a brat on the first day,” Lena says. Kara is leaning closer now, so close that they’re almost pressed together chest-to-chest with their joined hands caught between. “You can’t have liked me then.”

    “You were perfect. You are perfect.” Kara says it in a rasp, and Lena feels the words on her lips. Hot chocolate and cinnamon. “Lena.”

    Lena might never get tired of the way Kara says her name. It’s a statement and a question both, an admission and an exaltation, all wrapped up in those two syllables. Lena’s answer is in closing the distance.

    There’s no indecision to this kiss. Kara sweeps her up in an embrace that feels lyrical, picking up exactly where they left off, and Lena surrenders to it as soon as her back hits the door. It’s intense from the first moments – Kara takes as much as she gives, meeting Lena’s tongue halfway and pressing into her mouth in a deep, perfect expression of everything they’ve been holding back. Lena’s hands scramble for somewhere to land – she sinks them into Kara’s hair, tugging out what remains of her bun until it falls around Kara’s face in fragrant waves.     “You thought we’d wait for what?” Kara repeats. It’s to no avail – Winn disappears down the hall, and Kara has to jog to the door to yell after him.

    “Why does Winn owe Mike 60 bucks?”

    Lucy laughs, seemingly unbothered by Kara’s urgency.

    “Oh, did he admit defeat?” Lucy says. Her voice is distorted by the speakerphone, but she sounds amused. “Mike bet him that you two would finally get together before the show was over. I told Winn that last night was a clear sign, but he didn't want to give Mike the money yet.”

    Kara almost drops the phone.

    “What do you mean, finally?” Kara says. Her high, nervous laugh is not terribly convincing. Her grip is tight on the phone case, and her voice pitches up into a squeak. “We’ve been together for weeks!”

    Kara ends with a wince. Lena is fairly sure she knows how bad her lie sounds, and Lucy dismisses it easily.

    “Come on. I know you better than that.”

    Kara struggles to reply to that, and Lena is perfectly happy staying silent for the rest of the conversation – Lucy takes the silence as confirmation, and she continues happily.

    “Anyways, I’m happy for you two! I should throw a party. I’ll text you the plan, we can use Mike's place. It's the biggest.”

    Lucy hangs up without a goodbye. Kara blinks down at her phone screen as the call disconnects. The screen times out, going black and reflecting their faces peering down at it, and Kara stuffs it back in her pocket with a slight laugh.

    “How many of them do you think knew we weren’t actually dating?”

She and Kara arrive at Mike's apartment that evening later than everyone else – having gotten somewhat distracted in the process of grabbing clothes from Lena's hotel and then going back to Kara's to get ready – and Lena is still buzzing with post-quickie energy when the door opens to reveal the whole crew standing under a massive 'CONGRATULATIONS' banner.

    "Happy coming-out!" Alex shouts, raising her glass. Everyone else joins in, cheering and laughing as Kara stares aghast, her arm looped through Lena's.

    "I came out ten years ago?"

    "I don't think that's what they mean," Lena says quietly, the pure joyous energy of the gathering bringing a smile to her face even as she squirms under the attention.

    "I wanted the banner to say congratulations for fucking, but Alex intercepted it at the printer," Lucy says, taking a swig of champagne. Kelly hands a flute each to Lena and Kara, nudging Lucy's shoulder as she passes.

    "I don't think that would have made for a fun party for anyone but you."

    "Who says we've had sex?" Kara says defiantly. "You don't know that!"

    Lucy snorts into her drink. "Are you kidding? You're 45 minutes late. And you have two hickeys."

    "Fair point," Lena says, draining her champagne and pulling Kara by the hand towards the kitchen. She can see a bottle of scotch waiting on the island, and she gets the feeling the night will pass more smoothly with a few fingers of it.

    "Hey. For real," Alex says more quietly as they pass, catching Kara's elbow to intercept. "We're all really happy for you. The whole team has been rooting for you guys."

    "I don't understand how they all knew," Kara grumbles. "We put so much effort into it, and you were all just betting on us? I thought we were pretty convincing."

    Alex just grins. "Yeah, to the media maybe. But these guys all know you too well. Hell, half of them have dated you."

    "Not half!" Kara protests, as Lena leads her towards the kitchen laughing. Kara walks backwards, determinedly glaring at her sister. "Three people is not half, Alex!"

    The rest of the party ends up being far more pleasant than the shocking arrival. Everyone seems to be genuinely happy for them, just thrilled to celebrate their so-called coming out as an actual couple, and it's the first time Lena has felt truly comfortable expressing real affection outside the confines of Kara's apartment. She leans into Kara as they chat with the group, tangles their hands together as they sit on the couch, presses a kiss to her cheek when she hands Kara a bowl of chips – she even plops down in Kara's lap when she comes back from the bathroom to find her spot has been taken, and for once she doesn't have to convince herself it's only a performance. Kara just wraps her arms around Lena's waist, and presses a kiss to her cheek.

    It's only after Kara finally manages to fend off another round of karaoke and take their leave, when they've taken a cab back to Kara's place and collapsed into bed together around 3am, that Lena realizes she still hasn’t told Sam about this new development.

    Rolling over to grab her barely-charged phone from the nightstand, Lena scrolls to her text messages to find four unread from Sam and two from Jess, as well as a slew of emails she's been too distracted to glance at. She must have cleared the notifications at some point last night. One of Sam's looks to have been right after the broadcast, with the others coming later in the night when Lena had been otherwise occupied.

    Uhhh babe? You okay? What the hell just happened

    Was this kiss planned, or…?

    I am choosing to believe that you haven't answered for good reasons and not because you're sobbing in your hotel room

    Either way, call me in the morning

    Jess' messages are less frantic, but just as concerned.

    Miss Luthor, please text Miss Arias back before she has a stress induced health crisis.

    Also, please call me asap – lots of interview requests!

    Lena groans. She sends Sam a quick text to reassure her that she's okay and she'll call her tomorrow, and resolves to deal with Jess' interview requests then. For now, she needs nothing more than to curl into Kara's side and go to sleep.

     

     

     

     

    The hangover is thankfully minimal the next morning. Lena seems to sleep better at Kara's apartment than anywhere else in the world. Kara is awake early just like yesterday, already showered and drinking coffee when Lena drags herself out of bed, and she looks so good in her briefs and tank top, slightly transparent at the shoulders from her wet hair. Lena wishes dearly that they could start rehearsal late again purely so she could give in to her impulse to pull Kara back into bed. But today's rehearsal is different, and they're actually required to be there on time this morning.

    With so few pairs left in the competition to take up airtime, one of the usual finale time-fillers is a group number.

    If she'd been asked a few weeks ago to learn an entire second routine in a single rehearsal with Andrea and Siobhan in the room, she'd have given herself a stress migraine just thinking about it. But either her confidence in her own dancing ability has truly grown over the course of the show, or having the best sex of her life on Monday night affected her so fundamentally that she actually feels a bit cocky as she walks into the rehearsal studio with Kara. Either way, even the presence of other people doesn't shake Kara's words from her head. This week, we're gonna win the whole thing.

    Andrea and James have been arguably the most consistent pair in the competition, never skimming the bottom, but they've also never gotten the kind of glowing praise she and Kara have, with their most common critique being lack of chemistry. And Siobhan, while talented, is impatient and prone to tantrums backstage when she doesn't dance well. Not to mention the fact that she has Winn terrified of her rather than working as a team. She and Kara were eliminated, sure. But they've had higher highs than either of the pairs they're up against, and Lena is starting to share Kara's certainty that it can take them all the way to the top.

    When she and Kara stroll in promptly at 8:55 Andrea is leaning against the opposite wall in an animated phone conversation while James plays some kind of mobile game and Siobhan is doing stretches, her leg propped up on a miserable Winn's shoulder while she shows off her flexibility. Siobhan ignores them completely; Andrea gives Lena a hesitant smile and wave, which Lena returns.

    Lena hadn't really given a thought to who the choreographer might be. She's used to Kara being the one to create their numbers, so when Cat Grant strides confidently through the door in designer activewear with a team of assistants flanking her Lena almost asks why on earth she's here. Thankfully she manages to keep her mouth shut, but Siobhan does not.

    "You're our choreographer?" Siobhan says snidely. "Are you even a dancer?"

    Slowly, Cat slides her sunglasses off her face to pin her with a look so frosty that Siobhan actually takes a physical step back.

    "We have one day to bring this routine together," Cat says loudly to the room at large after a dramatic pause, not dignifying Siobhan with an answer. "After this rehearsal you're on your own. I expect it to be flawless on Monday night, so rehearsing it together is up to you."

    "No pressure," Andrea says under her breath.

    "This is the finale, Miss Rojas," Cat says, passing her coffee and sunglasses off to one of the assistants. "We are at the very pinnacle of pressure. Try not to buckle."

    Cat's routine is a short Jazz number, quick and energetic to start the grand finale off. It's only about half the length of their usual pairs routines, but easily twice as gruelling - it's split between sections where they dance in complete unison and sections meant to highlight each couple, giving them each a few moments to shine with a small series of lifts and flourishes. Lena surprises even herself by getting through their first full run without a single mistake, letting Kara throw her through the lifts without falling and somehow managing not to lose her footing as they transition back into the group steps, and Kara gives her a quick, enthusiastic kiss at the end of it that leaves Lena a little giddy with adrenaline.

    Siobhan, in true form, has complaints.

    "Why do they get the best part?" Siobhan snaps after they've gone through the full routine a few times, gesturing at Lena. "It's obviously favouritism. You've loved them since the beginning, and it shows."

    Lena wouldn't say their part is the best, necessarily – it might the most difficult, which could make it more eye-grabbing, but the whole dance is fun to look at in Lena's opinion. Siobhan and Winn get their moments just as much as the rest. She suspects that Siobhan's complaints are more born of insecurity than anything else. Now that only three pairs are left, any room for imperfection has disappeared.

    Cat whirls on Siobhan.

    "The choreography was decided long before the final 3, Miss Smythe," Cat says coldly. "If you can't find time to shine during your section, perhaps it's an issue of talent."

    "I have plenty of talent!" Siobhan says. Her lips are pulled almost in a snarl, but Cat remains as calm and collected as ever.

    "Oh? Well then," Cat says, her voice turning sickly sweet. "In that case. They got through a full run with no mistakes," Cat says, nodding towards where Kara is standing with her arm around Lena's shoulders. "If you and Mr. Schott can also perform the lifts you so covet in a single try, the part is yours."

    Siobhan's eye twitches.

    "Well?" Cat says impatiently, clapping twice. "Chop chop. Let's see you earn it."

    The tension crackles in the air for long moments. Siobhan looks about ready to burst a blood vessel, pure rage in the face of Cat's cool indifference. But in the end, she backs down.

    "Whatever," Siobhan huffs, as if it doesn't bother her in the slightest. "I guess I can make it work."

    Cat turns to Andrea and James. James shrinks back a little, but Andrea holds her ground admirably.

    "And what about you?" Cat says, giving Andrea a once-over. "Do you also need to whine like a petulant teenager, or can you prove your talent with what you're given?"

    Andrea raises an eyebrow. She's almost as cool-headed as Cat. "I have no complaints."

    Lena can practically feel the fury radiating from Siobhan from across the room.

    As the morning drags into afternoon and the number comes together with more precision, it becomes increasingly clear to Lena just how unique an experience she's had on the show so far. Just by watching Andrea and Siobhan interact with their partners she can see it – Siobhan and Winn very obviously detest each other, and their lack of trust comes across in the way they rehearse. Siobhan snaps at Winn for every mistake, and all it does is cause him to make even more errors. Andrea and James aren't nearly so antagonistic, but their relationship seems cordial at best. They run through the moves with impressive precision, but there truly is no emotion between them. They hardly even speak to each other during down-time. It's as if they're approaching dancing like a business deal. Lena can hardly imagine what it's like when they're rehearsing alone all week.

    In contrast, she and Kara have fun. They both pick the basics up fast enough that by mid-afternoon they're able to relax into it, and Lena doesn't mind occasionally messing up a step or missing a cue in front of everyone else because Kara is always there with a word of encouragement. She distracts Lena from the fact that they have an audience. The whole experience truly drives home how far Lena has come. After starting off struggling more than anyone else on the show, Lena now arguably struggles the least.

    Siobhan's bitter comments – said under her breath to avoid Cat's wrath - slide off Lena's back, for the most part. Andrea is much quieter through the rehearsal, more thoughtful than anything else, and when they finally wrap up around 6 she actually leaves James' side and approaches them as Kara is packing up.

    "Well done today," Andrea says, her hands clasped tightly in front of her. She's nervous, for some reason. Uncharacteristically so. "You two are going to be the stars of the show."

    "Hey, Andrea," Kara says, her tone carefully neutral as she slings Lena's bag and her own over each shoulder. "You did great, too. Looks like it's shaping up to be a fun number."

    "Looks that way," Andrea nods. It's distinctly awkward, and Lena gives her a small smile.

    "Did you need something?"

    "Yes, actually," Andrea says, smiling back at Lena but looking instead to Kara. "Kara, could I talk to you for a moment? Privately?"

    Kara blinks. Lena herself is just as shocked, and it must show on her face – Andrea almost laughs, waiting patiently for an answer. It seems good-natured, but Lena can't think of a reason for Andrea to want to talk to Kara except maybe to give her some kind of unnecessary shovel talk.

    "Really?" Kara says, the surprise in her voice not hidden at all. Andrea nods.

    "I won't take much of your time."

    "Uh," Kara says, letting the bags slide back to the ground. "Sure?"

    Lena is left with the bags while Kara follows Andrea to the corner of the room for a low conversation she can't hear. Kara looks defensive at first, arms crossed and ready for the worst, but gradually her expression softens – she still looks a bit confused, but in the end she's smiling as she shakes Andrea's hand. Andrea nods in Lena's direction before ducking out and heading downstairs alone, and Lena darts across the room to Kara as soon as she's gone.

    “What did she say to you?” Lena asks immediately, trying to decipher Kara's expression. Kara seems thoughtful, not upset.

    “Um,” Kara says, picking the bags up again and heading towards the door. “She thanked me?”

    Lena stops dead. Kara stops too, waiting for Lena to catch up, but Lena stays put.

    “Thanked you?" Lena says blankly. "Why?”

    “I’m not totally sure. She just said that she was glad we’d met each other, and she was sorry for how rude she was when we met. She complimented our dancing. Said she never thought she'd see you so happy again," Kara lists on her fingers. "And then she thanked me for, uh. Being what she couldn’t. Whatever that means.”

    Lena blinks slowly. "Oh."

    She shouldn't be so surprised, really – Andrea has clearly changed a lot in the years since their rift. Just a week ago Lena got a genuine apology and an offer of friendship from her, something that would have been deeply unlikely if not impossible when they were younger. It's not out of the question that this new, emotionally adjusted Andrea would wish them well and be something approaching kind to Kara.

    "And then she told me not to hurt you," Kara finishes with a grin. She reaches out for Lena's hand, interlacing their fingers and gently tugging Lena towards the stairs. "But she was surprisingly nice about the threats."

    Lena snorts. "That's more what I was expecting."

    "When she asked to talk to me I definitely thought it was going to go another way," Kara says. She swings their joined hands as they descend, squeezing Lena's fingers. "She used to glare at me pretty hard anytime we crossed paths."

    "She did admit to me last week that it's because she was jealous."

    "Hey!" Kara says brightly, pushing the door open and holding it for Lena to follow her to the street. Lena's driver is waiting, and Kara climbs into the car after her – she's been leaving her bike at home lately, since they've been carpooling every day. "I was right!"

    The day has been entirely too busy to call Sam like Lena had promised last night. While Kara gloats over her accurate reading of the Andrea situation Lena sends a quick text to confirm she's free now, and Sam's quick response is a string of emojis that seem mostly positive.

    "Back to my place?" Kara asks, buckling her seatbelt and looking pointedly at Lena until she does the same. Lena nods.

    "Yes. But…"

    Kara's face falls a little.

    "But?" Kara pouts. It's clearly playful, but it's still hard not to just buckle and put off her responsibilities again.

    "I need to call Sam."

    "Oh. Oh!" Kara says, her eyes widening. "Have you not told her about…?"

    "No," Lena confirms. "And the longer I hold off, the longer the gloating will last in retaliation. Besides, we have some logistics to talk over."

    Lena carefully avoids mentioning what those logistics are – such as Lena's return to Metropolis next week and her re-integration into CEO duties, neither of which Lena has particularly wanted to think about yet – and Kara doesn't ask. She chuckles, leaning back against the leather seat with her legs splayed in a thoughtless, absurdly attractive way that makes it even more difficult to not just follow her up to her apartment and strip her of her sweatpants.

    "Do you want to come over after?" Kara asks easily. Lena nods again, much more happily this time.

    "Gladly."

    She drops Kara off at her apartment with a kiss – several, actually, until Kara has her pressed against the side of the car and Lena has to restrain herself and get back in before she traumatizes her poor driver – and as soon as she closes the car door again she dials Sam's number.

    Sam answers on the first ring.

    "Dr. Luthor, I presume?"

    "I have some news," Lena says, blowing right past the reference. Sam knows she doesn't like being referred to by her doctorates – it reminds her too much of Lex, constantly lording his accolades over others – but she has more important things to deal with at the moment.

    "Uh huh," Sam says. Lena takes a deep breath.

    "Kara and I…well, things have changed a little."

    "Right."

    "We've…well, we're sort of –" Lena stutters, wincing in frustration. She hates having to search for the right words for this sort of thing. Ask her to synthesize scientific theory and she can do it in a heartbeat, but she struggles to voice something so simple. "After the show last week…"

    "Honey, this isn't a murder confession," Sam says, cutting through Lena's tangled words gently. "You can just tell me you're finally dating her."

    Lena lets out all her breath. It's almost a relief having Sam take the reins, but it's also slightly galling.

    "How did you –"

    "Alex told me."

    "Okay, since when have you two been friends?" Lena huffs. It's been overshadowed by other things since, but now that Sam mentions Alex the memory returns to her – Alex had mentioned Sam too, just before Lena left to tell Kara how she felt. She'd meant to ask about it, but much like remembering to talk to Sam, most of her thoughts have been tossed to the wayside in favour of Kara lately.

    "Since I came to visit you," Sam says. "She and her wife agreed with me that you two were being absolutely ridiculous and were clearly barrelling towards a messy misunderstanding. We bonded over being constantly correct and yet tragically ignored."

    "Maybe I wouldn't ignore you if you weren't such an ass about it."

    "Such an ass about what?" Another voice chimes in distantly. It's a familiar voice, but once Lena wasn't expecting to hear.

    "About Lena finally accepting the obvious," Sam calls back.

    "That's not Ruby. Is that Jess?" Lena frowns, twisting her arm to look at her watch as the car pulls up to her hotel. "You aren't still at the office, are you? Isn't it almost 10pm in Metropolis?"

    "No, I'm not at the office," Sam says. "Not all of us live to work. I'm on my couch."

    "And Jess is with you? So you two are just…hanging out, now?" Lena asks. She waves back at the familiar concierge as she passes through the lobby, stepping into an empty elevator and heading up to her floor.

    "As a matter of fact, yes."

    "I'm here too!" Ruby chimes in.

    "And Ruby's here too. The experience of trying to run this place without you has bonded us," Sam says. Her voice sounds tinny, like she's switched Lena to speakerphone. "Have I told you how glad I am that you're coming back soon? Your job is hell."

    "Language, mom!"

    "We're watching Gilmore Girls," Jess says loudly over the mother-daughter banter. "So what's the obvious?" Jess' voice gets noticeably perkier, and Lena can hear something pouring. Probably wine, knowing Sam. "Oh! Miss Luthor, did you and Kara finally –"

    "Yes, they finally," Sam interjects. "Fi-na-lly."

    "Oh!" Jess squeals. Lena can picture her reaction, hands flapping excitedly over Lena's previously non-existent love life. "Did my business suggestion get Miss Luthor a girlfriend?!"

    "Apparently," Lena drawls. The elevator dings and Lena steps out, fiddling with her keycard. "I'm as shocked as you are."

    "I'm not shocked," Sam says. "But I'm going to hold back from asking the 300 very prying questions I want to ask, and just stick to one." Her voice softens a little. "Are you happy?"

    Lena's door opens with a soft beep, and Lena steps inside. The lights are dim, most of Lena's things thrown haphazardly around – she's only been here a few times in the last few days, and usually just to brush her teeth and grab clothes in the morning before rehearsal. It almost feels a bit foreign to be here without Kara.

    Just thinking about Kara makes her feel warm and contented, even though they parted ways less than half an hour ago.

    "Unbelievably."

    "Good," Sam says. Lena can hear the smile in her voice. "I know I tease, but that's the most important thing. You know that, right?"

    "I do," Lena says, flopping back on the bed. "And because I care, if you really want to ask prying questions, I will give you 3."

    "Call it a challenge."

    "Okay. Ruby, cover your ears for a sec," Sam says, and Lena hears the gentle clink of two glasses tapping together in a toast. Sam's voice gets low and conspiratorial. "Question one. How was it?"

    Lena had guessed that this might be the first inquiry, and she has an answer prepared to lob back right away.

    "Life-changing."

    "I knew it!" Sam crows. "I knew from the second I saw her she'd be dynamite in bed. Especially with how obsessed with you she is. I bet she didn't let up until you passed out."

    "Question two," Sam says, while Jess giggles in the background. "Yes, Ruby, you can listen now - what happened after the show? We saw you two kiss on stage, and I knew from your face that something was going to go down. You were on another planet during the final 3 announcement."

    Lena hums. "I was. When we got backstage Kara told me how she felt, and I froze up."

    "Oh, no."

    "I know," Lena says, shaking her head with a light chuckle. She already feels so far away from that moment that it feels like a previous life. "Alex talked some sense into me. So I went to Kara's place, and we…sorted things out."

    "I bet you did," Sam says, her voice taking on a lecherous undertone. Lena rolls her eyes, but she does admire Sam's relative restraint. It must be because Ruby is in the room.

    "Question three?" Lena says pointedly.

    "Question three," Sam confirms, chuckling along with Jess. "What are you guys going to do after this week? Kara gets some time off after the show, right? Will she be gracing us with her presence?"

    That gives Lena pause.

    It's not that she hasn't considered the future. Part of the reason she'd tried so hard not to fall for Kara is the fact that they live on opposite sides of the country. She knows intellectually that the end of the show means going back to Metropolis, and though Kara does get some time off in the off-season it's limited. And she and Kara haven't discussed what they plan to do. She's been enjoying living in this happy bubble for a little while, and Sam's question is like a cold slap in the face.

    Their relationship has only just started, really. And it's going to need to hinge on a massive amount of distance. Lena isn't well-versed in making relationships work even in close proximity.

    What if what Lena can offer isn't good enough?

    "That's two extra questions," Lena says tightly. The anxiety is starting to gnaw at her, and Sam picks up on it immediately.

    "Lena," Sam says, her tone getting less blasé and more careful. "Your voice just got weird."

    "My voice is fine."

    "You just went from happy to anxious," Sam points out with the devastating accuracy of a best friend. "And I'm going to guess that it's because I asked about what happens after the finale."

    Lena doesn't reply.

    "Have you two talked about it?" Sam asks.

    "No. We haven't."

    "Are you going to talk about it?" Jess pipes in. She might not know Lena quite so intimately as Sam does, but her perceptiveness can't be underestimated.

    "Eventually."

    Lena knows she's being terse. But it's hard to talk when everything she's been avoiding is coming home to roost, and Sam gives her a gentle push.

    "Well, what do you want? What's your ideal scenario?"

    "I want her," Lena says. It hardly takes a thought. There, at least, Lena doesn't have doubts anymore.

    "But?"

    "What if doing cross-country long-distance ends up being too much for her? For both of us? It would be exhausting. With her shooting schedule and my absurd working hours…"

    "The flights between Metropolis and National City are really frequent, and only about 6 hours on average without layovers," Jess says, ever the organized planner. "It's not unreasonable!"

    "There are also alternatives," Sam points out.

    "And they have downsides too," Lena argues. "If I move here, I'll have to either uproot the entire company or leave L-Corp. Everything I've worked for for years. I've spent so long turning it around. I mean, this whole ridiculous reality show business was meant to help the company. I can't just leave. I don't want to leave."

    "You don't have to. What if she came to Metropolis?"

    "I don't want to rip her away from her life in National City," Lena says heavily. "Her job, her family. And I don't even know if she'd want to move across the country just for me. We technically only started dating yesterday. I don't know what I can offer her that would be worth that."

    "You know what might help?" Sam says matter-of-factly. Lena groans.

    "I know I need to talk to her. I just need to think first," Lena says, rolling over to press her face into the duvet. "Otherwise I'll end up saying something stupid and hurting her like I did Monday night."

    She hangs up a few minutes later, and after a few minutes staring up at the ceiling to process the conversation and consider Sam's words she finally heaves herself up to change into jeans and grab some essentials to shove in her bag. She doesn't intend on sleeping here this week, and she's getting tired of having to stop by every morning just to brush her teeth.

    Her phone starts to ring just as she's leaving the hotel, and she picks up with a smile when she sees Kara's contact photo.

    "Miss me already?"

    "Always," Kara says easily. "Done talking to Sam?"

    "Just finished. I'm on my way over now, if that's okay?" Lena's driver doesn't even need to be told where to go – he pulls into traffic towards Kara's, and Lena fastens her seatbelt.

    "About that," Kara says, just as Lena hears other voices in the background. Laughter, and several people by the sounds of it. "Um. Do you want to meet my foster mom?"

    Lena blinks at her own reflection in the window.

    "Do I…what?"

    "Alex and Kelly came over without warning a couple minutes ago and it turns out Eliza decided to make a surprise visit from Midvale," Kara says, quick and apologetic. "I totally get if you're not ready for that, so if that's the case I can let you know when they go home. She's staying with Alex."

    "Eliza is there right now?" Lena clarifies.

    "She's currently making kugel in my kitchen," Kara says. "Again, it's totally fine if you're not comfortable! I wanted to give you the choice so you didn't get ambushed when you got here."

    Lena takes a deep breath. If anything can act as a litmus test for the strength of her resolve to put aside her fears, it's meeting a family member as important to Kara as Eliza. 

    "No, it's okay. I want to meet her," Lena says.

    "Are you sure?"

    "I am. Besides," Lena says, steeling her nerves, "I've never had kugel."

    Eliza is exactly as warm and welcoming as Lena expected from someone who helped to raise Kara. She's nothing like Lillian, who has always been Lena's only real touchpoint for female guardians – Lillian had been cold and cruel, withholding affection and approval even as Lena bent herself backwards to please her. Eliza is wonderful. She meets Lena with a genuine smile and a tight hug, immediately bringing Lena to the kitchen to share the secrets behind a perfect kugel, and it isn't until they've all sat down to eat together that Lena realizes there's been a massive gap in communication.

    Kara's smile is sunlight. Lena kisses her again; she tastes like ranch dip and beer, and Lena loves her more than ever.

     

     

     

     

    Finale day starts with dappled sun on Lena's bare skin, the smell of fresh coffee, and warm lips kissing a path up the column of her spine.

    "Morning," Lena rasps, stretching out on her stomach in the sunbeam and humming happily when Kara settles on top of her. Kara mumbles against her skin.

    "Sleep well?"

    "I think in this week alone I've caught up on a decade's worth of bad sleep," Lena drawls. She can feel Kara's smile against her shoulder.

    "Good. I'm hoping you have lots of energy left over for after the show tonight. I've heard that victory sex is top-notch."

    Lena giggles, shifting insistently until Kara lifts herself up in a plank and lets her roll over underneath her. Kara leaves the space between them, flexing and grinning when Lena's eyes go right to her biceps, and with a groan Lena pulls her down by the waistband of her sweats for a kiss.

    "Remind me to find a good gym when I move to Metropolis," Kara says between kisses, doing a few quick push-ups interspersed with kisses. "Clearly I need to keep these up to keep you interested."

    "You just want to watch me lift."

    "As is my right as your girlfriend," Lena says lightly. "I get full access to your sweaty flexing."

    "You haven't called yourself that before," Kara says. "My girlfriend. At least not without the word fake in front of it."

    It's true, now that she thinks about it; despite multiple declarations of love and commitment at this point and a tentative plan to fully relocate across the country, they haven't actually put words to this yet. Girlfriend. It had slipped out without much thought, but now seems so meaningful.

Evidently she's furious about it covering her shoulders, ranting to Alex about the tragedy of obscuring her best feature when Andrea and Lena's dresses don't, and as Lena slips past the argument with Kara in tow she finds herself surprisingly wistful about every element of what's happening backstage; Alex's flustered argument with Siobhan, the constant flurry of technicians and assistants, the cramped hallways, and Kara guiding her through it all with a hand on her lower back. Even the smell of the auditorium is strangely comforting, like old velvet seats and dust burning on hot lights.

Kara is still in darkness as the first violin notes quiver into being – Lena's movements are stiff and elegant, almost ballet-like, and pulled directly from Lena's childhood lessons. They follow those two low repeating notes as the cameras swing wide to catch Kara's spotlight turning on just as the faster, higher string overtones start to faintly blend in.

She dances in a wide circle around the back of the stage, her loose contemporary-style steps and jumps leading her towards Lena in an arc as she follows the more inconsistent higher notes of the intro; they’re dancing to the same song and yet a different one at the same time, different elements of the same melody leaving them in different tempos. Lena slow and precise, Kara relaxed and eager; Kara approaching, offering, and Lena holding back. Just like their initial partnership, it shouldn't work as well as it does. But Lena can feel it hitting home anyways.

    Kara dances closer and closer as the violins swell, her eyes focused completely on Lena alone in her shaft of light, until the moment Kara hits centre stage. The song sweeps into a huge crescendo; Lena's slower moves turn into an approximation of a jeté as she runs at Kara, and when every other instrument swells up to meet the violins they crash together and barrel straight into the heart of it.

    It's the most carefree Lena has ever felt on stage. Her comfort level has been steadily rising week by week with Kara's help, culminating in the exhilaration of last week's performance, but with all the pressure off now it feels easier than ever to let go and allow the flow of the routine to sweep her up.

    They hit every beat together with ease. Integrated into the sweeping freestyle are relaxed elements from each of their previous routines, held together by contemporary movements that tell the story of their progression – a lift from their tango, spins from their cha-cha and samba, a quick dip from their waltz. A short section where they dance away from each other, pulled apart by forces beyond them, only to crash together again with a few hard, whip-fast steps from their paso doble. A spinning lift where their lips drift so close together that it likely looks like a kiss from the perspective of the cameras, during which the crowd goes wild. Each element is looser and more playful than the routine it was pulled from, but Lena can hear in the shifting reaction of the crowd that the intention is recognized. The whole thing is an expression of love. An exercise in mutual vulnerability.

    Lena can't wipe the smile off her face from the moment they first touch, but luckily it suits the routine – Kara is beaming too, radiating love and pride as they cover the whole stage together. And it only grows as they ease into their ending lift.

    If there were a single element in this routine that Lena might have felt nervous about, it would be this. It's the longest and most intensive lift they've ever done, the primary focus of the whole latter half of their rehearsal week – for close to 20 whole seconds Lena's feet hardly touch the ground, relying entirely on her own balance and Kara's strong grip as she's swung around by ankle and wrist, twisted around and under Kara's legs, and then lifted into two different spinning configurations before sliding down Kara's body face-to-face to be set gently on the ground while the song fades out. But when she searches the place those nerves usually live, she finds nothing but trust.

    So she holds tight to Kara's hand, and takes flight.

    Maybe it's the weeks of practice, or maybe there really is something special about this routine – but somehow, it feels like the easiest lift they've ever done. She feels weightless in Kara's hands. She feels like a piece of art in motion; she feels beautiful.

    It isn't meant to end in a kiss. But when the perfectly-executed lift ends and the music fades into deafening screams Kara is clinging to her, sweaty and cry-laughing and absurdly, heart-wrenchingly beautiful, and Lena is only human. It's brief, half-overtaken by smiling and salty tears, but it settles her enough to pull away and be led towards the judging table as the auditorium seems to vibrate in its foundations with cheers.

    "Wow," William says, the first to speak when the crowd settled down enough that anyone can be heard through it. "Wow, wow, wow. Kara, Lena…it's finale night, and you brought us nothing short of pure joy."

    The crowd cheers reliably loudly, and it gives both of them a chance to wipe their faces – Lena is sure her makeup is smudged from both tears and Kara's lips, but she doesn't care.

    "It is my job to find words to describe people’s performances on this stage, but I…I have no words," William continues, heedless for once of the crowd reaction. His attention is purely on them. "The song choice. The connection you share. The bliss you two find in each other and the way you share it with the audience? You two are what dance is all about. You elevate the medium.”

    Kara is squeezing Lena's hand so tight that she's lost most sensation in her fingers. But she squeezes back, leaning into her while Jonn nods his agreement.

    “Absolutely agreed, William. It has been a privilege to have a front seat to watch you two grow this season. And to culminate with something so…beautiful.” Jonn's eyes, even from this distance, almost look shiny. “Wonderful job, ladies. Truly a perfect routine to end your run here, no matter the results of tonight's vote.”

    Cat, as usual, waits until the crowd has gone almost silent to speak.

    “I said from day one that you two could be a powerhouse in this competition. I do love being right.”

    The crowd flares up in response, and Cat waits again with a look of immense satisfaction. “I don’t think we’ll see another pairing like this one for a long time. Maybe never again on this show." She smiles, and together with the slight gloating of a shot well-called it also feels almost proud. Like a mentor watching her beloved wards succeed, despite the fact that Lena has never actually had a prolonged conversation with the woman. “Good luck tonight, ladies. And I truly mean that.”

    Kara hugs her fiercely the moment they get backstage. They have a commercial break and a scheduled music performance to fill the latter half of the show and give the fan votes time to come in, and it allows Lena to process what ends up being their third clean sweep of 10's across the board from the judges. Even though it's at least 15 minutes between their exit from the stage and their re-emergence to hear the final result, it feels like it flies by in a whirlwind of hair and makeup and thrilling anticipation.

    It all slows back down when they're in their places. The three pairs are lined up behind the trophy, which is on a brightly-lit stand waiting to be taken by the winners. The lights are unthinkably bright, the crowd unthinkably huge – each of Lena's heartbeat seems to take twice as long, and she can tell from Kara's twitchy body language that she's struggling not to sprint at Brainy and rip the envelope out of his hands to read it herself. And Lena shares the sentiment – she's usually amused by Brainy's grandstanding, but right now the wait for him to read the names is agonizing.

    "The couple in third place, is…"

    Brainy takes the paper out with a flourish. He holds it out in front of him, making a show of reading it, before sweeping an arm to his right.

    "Siobhan and Winn."

    Siobhan flinches. She tries to hide it, clapping politely in a way that contrasts deeply with the enraged look in her eyes, but Lena is fairly sure the cameras caught her moment of weakness – the crowd claps too, and while Brainy congratulates them for a well-fought season both dancers shake his hand before heading backstage to leave the final 2 to their fate.

Kara is pressed against her left side and Andrea is only a few feet away to her right, and as Brainy opens the envelope Andrea catches her eye and gives her a smile. It's a proud sort of expression, as if she's already celebrating for Lena.

    "Andrea and James."

    Everything goes fuzzy. Kara's arm tightens around her waist, and then squeezes hard –

    They won.

    "Which means that Kara and Lena are our winners, and the new champions of Dancing with the Stars!" Brainy booms, throwing the envelope into the air. "Congratulations, and thank you Andrea and James for a fantastic effort -"

There are pyrotechnics going off, confetti falling, balloons descending from the ceiling onto the audience, but Lena is somewhere outside her body – as if the wall of vocal approval and Kara's delighted shouts are lifting her her up like a kite. Lena clings to her, trying to sort through the maelstrom of emotions she's feeling – joy, disbelief, excitement, shock, love. Lena didn't make a fool of herself, and she didn't stumble at the finish line. They actually won.

    "Sorry, what was the question?"

    Kara has to shout into the mic just to be heard over the crowd. Lena's ears are ringing. Kara has confetti in her hair.

She thanks the fans and the judges and praises Lena with every second word, and when the trophy is handed to her she immediately gives it to Lena to hold up for the cameras and the screaming audience. The judges are standing up behind their panel desk, clapping for them - Lena could swear she sees Cat Grant wipe at her eyes.

They don't even begin to quiet until Kara and Lena are shuffled backstage, where Lena is met instead with the entire crew swarming around them in a wave of chaotic congratulations.     "You seemed like you needed a rescue," Alex drawls, stepping back with a little smile. "Congratulations. You guys really earned it."

    "Oh," Lena says. A small thread of anxiety worms its way into her bubble of happiness.

    "I just have one question."

    "Which is?"

    Alex makes a show of looking thoughtful. "How nice is your spare bedroom?"

    Lena laughs. Relief and affection for Alex pull the thread free and toss it to the wind – they've had their differences, but at the end of the day Alex's concern is always for Kara's happiness. Just like Lena's.

    Alex grins. "That's acceptable."

She seems to have noticed Lena's absence, and while Mike ruffles her hair and Lucy grabs the trophy and kisses it her eyes flicker around until they land on Lena again. Her grin is so bright, full and unburdened and a little lopsided, and Alex chuckles quietly.

    Lena is pretty sure Kara would be shocked to find out just how fast she could come right now.

Their clothes for the afterparty are in Kara's bag, and Lena wants to be out of this leotard as quickly as possible – she grabs them and changes in the bathroom before Alex can ask why they're still here, and when she emerges leotard-less with her hair finally free of bobby pins in her low-necked clubbing dress she even manages to survive being kissed with devastating heat the moment Kara finds her again. She glances at Lena and then at the door countless times, even going so far as to announce that she's getting tired before the clock has even hit 11pm, but it's to no avail. People keep waylaying them; wanting to talk and offer congratulations, wanting to take selfies, wanting to ask how they feel about their win. How Lena feels is that if she doesn't have Kara's clothes off within the hour she might go out of her mind, but if she's being honest she’s also enjoying watching Kara get more visibly worked up with each interception.     Realization hits Lena like a bolt of lightning.

    "The Four Seasons on Jepson, please?" Kara says to the driver, and Lena pauses in her quest to kiss her way up the side of Kara's neck.

    “Not your place?”

    “You’re going back to Metropolis tomorrow night, right?" Kara says, grinning. Her pulse point is stained with Lena's lipstick shade already. "We need to give your suite a proper send-off.”
